# AMAZON.COM, INC. — STAGE 3 CLAIM-RECORDS APPENDIX (SECTIONS A–U)

**Dataset:** THE FOUNDER'S PLAYBOOK · **Company:** Amazon.com, Inc. (company_001) · **Stage:** 3
(1997-05-16 → **endpoint under argument**; work carried to 1999-12-31) · **Role:** claim-record appendix to
`stage_3_part_1.md` (§Header, boundary, A–J) / `stage_3_part_2.md` (§K–O) / `stage_3_part_3.md` (§P–U,
U.114–U.168), one record per material claim, per `00_METHOD_AND_STYLE.md` §7.

**Provenance of the text.** This file is mined from the three Stage-3 narrative volumes, from the five
Stage-3 dossiers under `research/` (`ST3_A_chronology_org.md` S3A-\* (59), `ST3_B_finance.md` S3B-\*
(22 records / 142 register rows), `ST3_C_product_market.md` S3C-\* (68), `ST3_D_tech_ops.md` S3D-\* (53),
`ST3_E_adversarial.md` S3E-\* (37 attacks + 10 held)), from the 101 local documents under `sources/`
**including the FY1999 Form 10-K (acc. 0000891020-00-000622, filed 2000-03-23) and the FY1999 10-K/A
(acc. 0000891020-00-001638, filed 2000-09-08), which arrived after most dossiers closed** and are cited here
where they fill a null (method §14.11), and from the nine registers (`sources.csv`, `quantitative.csv`,
`validation.csv`, `failures.csv`, `conflicts.csv`, `decisions.csv`, `channels.csv`, `timeline.csv`,
`data_gaps.csv`) read-only at Stage-3 rows `stage3`. **No web request was made in the construction of this
file**; every retrieval need a dossier leaves unmet is recorded **UNTRIED** with its query, never as a null
about the record. Nothing in the narrative was upgraded, rounded, merged or re-classed here: every Date /
Source / Source date / Passage / Conf / Corroboration field is the specialist's or the filing's own string,
and `UNKNOWN` stands where it was recorded. Where narrative and dossier disagree, the disagreement is
recorded and reported; it is not fixed (§14 rule 4), and it is listed under `## Defects noticed`.

**Id continuity (method §9.3).** Record ids **continue the company sequence and are never re-based**, so that
every record id in the folder is globally unique. Stage 2 closes at A08, B124, C40, D34, E48, F41, G49, H44,
I35, J35, K34, L20, M26, N19, O17, P90, Q69, R25, S30, T40 and **U.44 → U.113** (plus the U.111a addendum).
This file therefore opens at **A09, B125, C41, D35, E49, F42, G50, H45, I36, J36, K35, L21, M27, N20, O18,
P91, Q70, R26, S31, T41** and carries the Stage-3 conflict spine **U.114 → U.168** (55 blocks).
§A–§D below are the 79 records the narrative drafts at `stage_3_part_1.md` l.713 ff. under their **provisional
narrative ids A01–A26 / B01–B20 / C01–C13 / D01–D20**; the mapping is a pure offset (**A +8, B +124, C +40,
D +34**) applied so ids stay globally unique — no record text was re-cut, and the provisional id is recoverable
from the offset. The volumes keep their own numbering and are not edited here. §E–§U records are authored from
the volumes and the dossiers, and each names the line it was read at.

**Conflict spine.** Every `Conflicts:` reference is keyed to the canonical Stage-3 register **U.114–U.168**
(55 blocks ↔ 55 Stage-3 rows in `conflicts.csv`), stated in full at §U of `stage_3_part_3.md`. The
narrative's provisional keys `P-U.nnn` are the same blocks and appear here as `U.nnn`
(`stage_3_part_3.md` §"CONFLICT RE-KEY MAP": 39 re-keyed + 16 newly emitted; dossier originals S3A U.201–U.211,
S3B C-1…C-9, S3C U-C1…U-C12, S3D U.D1…U.D8 are echoed in each §U record so a dossier row still resolves).
Stage 1 owns U.1–U.43 and Stage 2 U.44–U.113; those are cited as `(S1 U.n)` / `(S2 U.n)`. A key that could not
be mapped is printed `UNMAPPED — see coverage note`, never guessed.

**Filing-lineage rule applied to every `Corroboration:` field (method §3; Stage-1 AUDIT 5 A-B1 / RD-041;
Stage-2 header).** Three lineages bind this stage, and each is **one source**, not several:
(1) the **IPO registration statement, File No. 333-23795** — S-1 original (acc. 0000891618-97-001309,
1997-03-24), its **six** amendments (Nos. 1–6, acc. 0000891020-97-000603, -000659, -000755, -000822, -000839,
-000847) and the 424B1 final prospectus (acc. 0000891020-97-000868, 1997-05-15) — which Stage 3 cites only as
*pre-stage state and as the split-vintage denominator*;
(2) **a Form 10-K and its own exhibits are one filing**, so an exhibit's restatement of an Item 6 figure is not
a second witness; and
(3) the load-bearing one for this stage: **FY1999 Form 10-K (acc. 0000891020-00-000622, filed 2000-03-23) and
FY1999 Form 10-K/A (acc. 0000891020-00-001638, filed 2000-09-08) are ONE DOCUMENT RESTATED** — same registrant,
same auditor, same accession family (0000891020-00-), the amendment being the instrument's second state.
Agreement between them records `Corroboration: 1 (same lineage: the FY1999 10-K and its 10-K/A are one document
restated)`, never 2.
**The asymmetry the rule cuts both ways on:** lineage members are one *source* but they can differ as
*versions*, and a version difference is a **conflict to register, not corroboration to claim** — hence
**U.154** (FY1998's change in cash +23,685 in the 10-K against (38,536) in the 10-K/A), **U.157** (the 10-K/A's
"18 million shares" / 36:1 note restated through two of three splits), and **U.164** (FY1998 10-K exhibit F2
"not restated for the stock split" against that same 10-K's restated Item 6). Where a lineage's later state
supplies content the earlier lacks, that is a **new disclosure of the same instrument**, cited with its own
accession and date. **No confidence level moves on a re-key** — §3 grants High on a primary document alone —
so what the rule protects is the count and the "sources per claim" tally a later cross-company pass inherits.
Draft records carried in from the narrative with a count that rested on lineage-internal agreement were
re-keyed under this rule and the superseded string is left visible in the cell.

**`(PB)` discipline and the CONTESTED endpoint (method §2; Stage-3 boundary).** The Stage-2 boundary is frozen
at **1997-05-15** and nothing after it is Stage-2 state; inside Stage 3 the converse hazard is the *close*, and
**Stage 3's endpoint is not settled: 1997-12-31, 1999-06-30, 1999-09-30 and 1999-12-31 are all argued** in
`stage_3_part_1.md` §STAGE BOUNDARY JUSTIFICATION (three positions, with Position A's load-bearing claim
falsified three times over) and §J.6. **This appendix therefore works to 1999-12-31 — the widest candidate —
but does not assume it.** Every record whose *status* (in-window state vs `(PB)` consequence vs post-window
re-witnessing) changes under a narrower close is flagged **`[ENDPOINT-SENSITIVE: <what changes under
1997-12-31 / 1999-06-30 / 1999-09-30>]`** in its own cell, and §J.6's sensitivity table is registered row by row
at §J; the enumeration of affected records is restated in the `## Coverage note`. Material dated after the
working close is tagged `(PB)` exactly as the narrative tags it and is admissible only as a consequence of the
stage. The FY1999 10-K and 10-K/A are `(PB)` documents **for the window they describe** and Tier-1 primary
carriers **for their own assertions** — the two roles are kept apart in every record that cites them.

**`Passage:` convention and what was re-read on this pass (method §14 rule 3).** A `Passage:` cell is one of:
(1) an **unmarked quotation** — the witness's own words in one contiguous run, present in a local document at
the line the `Source:` field names; (2) **`[paraphrase — not the filing's wording]`** — the content is what the
cited line says, the words are this appendix's; (3) **`[table composite, figures filed]`** — the values are on
the cited line but not in this contiguous order, so the string is a reading of a table and never a sentence the
document contains; (4) **`[narrative/dossier string, not re-read at line on this pass]`** — carried verbatim
from the volume or dossier that printed it with its line anchor, which is where the quotation was verified and
where this pass did not re-verify it; (5) `NO_VERBATIM_PASSAGE_RECORDED` — the drafter recorded the claim
without a string, and it stays a gap rather than being filled by invention. Markers are applied only to cells
this pass actually classified: **a decorative marker is itself a defect.** Quotations re-run against `sources/`
by grep on this pass are individually marked **`re-read here`** with the file and line; where a re-read did not
land, the record says so and keeps the string it inherited.

**Line-keying declaration (`research/_EVIDENCE_CACHE.md` "THE LINE-KEYING RULE FOR `l.NNNN`", AUDIT-2 DEFECT-6
repair).** Two accessions exist in two local copies and the offset is a **constant 17 lines**: every
`l.NNNN` / `LNNNN` reference in this appendix keys to the **convention-named, headered copy** —
`sources/S-1_original_acc-0000891618-97-001309_filed-1997-03-24.txt` and
`sources/S-1A-No5_acc-0000891020-97-000839_filed-1997-05-14.txt` — **not** to the bare twins
`s1_original_0000891618-97-001309.txt` (27,459 lines) and `s1_0000891020-97-000839.txt` (5,103 lines), which
lack the Level-3 Registrar's 17-line `PROVENANCE HEADER`. **Conversion: twin line = keyed line − 17.** The
four further Stage-3 duplicates (`S-1A_No1/2/4/6_…`, underscore beside hyphen) are byte-identical and carry no
offset. Grepping the twin and reading a keyed line number produces ~900 phantom citation failures; an auditor
who finds one should apply this conversion before reporting it.

**Restatement pairs, not single values (method §14.9/§14.11; graded rule).** No figure in this appendix is
printed as a single value where the record holds two states. **The cumulative split factor inside Stage 3 is
12× — FY1999 10-K l.1691-1692 lists "the 2-for-1 stock split effected June 1, 1998, the 3-for-1 stock split
effected January 4, 1999, and the 2-for-1 stock split effected September 1, 1999": 2 × 3 × 2 = 12 — while the
3-for-2 of 1997-04-18 (l.3817) falls **before** Stage 3 opens on 1997-05-16 and must not be multiplied in; from
the 1995 basis the cumulative factor is 72×. §K.8's own vintage table is registered at K-series and any
variance between it and these four dates is printed as a conflict, not resolved here.** Registered pairs printed with both witnesses and the document named:
**FY1996** net loss **$(5,777)k as-filed** vs **$(6,246)k as re-printed**; **FY1997** net sales **$147,758k
as-filed** (10-K405) vs **$147,787k as restated** (FY1998 10-K, pooling recast); **FY1997 net loss $(27,590)k
as-filed** vs **$(31,020)k restated**; **FY1998 net sales $609,996k as-filed** vs **$609,819k as re-printed in
the FY1999 10-K**; **FY1998 change in cash +23,685 (10-K) vs (38,536) (10-K/A)**; and **1997/98 technology
spend and depreciation, which have NO single filed value** (U.158) and are recorded as the absence, not as a
number. Where a value is quoted from one witness only, the record names the other witness and the direction of
the difference.

**Retracted values stay retracted (graded rule).** The following may appear in this file **only inside a
retraction sentence**, and none of them is used as a value: `2,613,000` and `$871,000` / `$871,024`
(S2E-28 / Stage-2 U.107, **re-detected at Stage 3 as instruction-layer residue and registered as U.168**);
`$976,408`; the fabricated FY1996 set `12,284 / 3,462 / 4,322 / 850 / 1,326 / (3,036) / 2,398 / 3,268 / 8,839 /
5,804`; and the false cash bridge `52 − 232 − 52 + 1,228`. Every such string present in this appendix sits in a
sentence that withdraws it.

**Register ids and the RD-078 merge collision (graded rule).** ~219 Stage-3 rows in the registers carry
`source_id`s made **unresolvable by a merge collision (RD-078)**: one id maps to more than one document, so a
bare id cannot be joined back to a filing. **Consequently no record in this appendix cites a bare ambiguous
register id as its locator.** Every citation is **document + line** (`sources/<convention-named file>`,
accession, filed date, `l.NNNN` in the keyed copy's numbering); a `[src: …]` key is a *provenance trace* into
the dossiers and registers, not a locator, and where a register row cannot currently be joined the record says
**`register row unjoinable (RD-078)`** rather than implying the join succeeds. `03_quality_control/
stage3_register_binding.md` and `03_quality_control/stage3_sourceid_rekey_map.md` are the governing repairs;
this appendix does not edit the registers.

**Coverage obligation (checked in the `## Coverage note`, gaps named not smoothed).** (i) every §U block
**U.114–U.168** (55) recorded with **both sides**, sources and dates; (ii) every **DERIVED** figure in §P /
§P.2 with its **arithmetic record**; (iii) every table row in §K–§N and every §L / §M row with a record;
(iv) §Q timeline rows, §R snapshot rows, §S gaps, §T provenance rows. A stated shortfall is a deliverable; a
silent gap is a defect.

A. EXECUTIVE STATE SUMMARY — CLAIM RECORDS

*Format per method §7. Line anchors are `sources/` files, in the keyed copy's numbering (see the line-keying
declaration above). `[src: …]` is the provenance trace into the Stage-3 dossiers and registers.*

E. PRODUCT RECONSTRUCTION (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

*Format per method §7. The unit of evidence for Stage-3 product history is **the filing, not the decision**
(narrative §E.0): rows keep the narrative's states — DECLARED / LAUNCHED / OPERATING AT SCALE / NOT
ESTABLISHED — and a forward-tense sentence never fills LAUNCHED.*
**Standing reading limit for §E (E49–E95): the `Passage:` cells below are the strings the narrative prints with
their `sources/` line anchors, transcribed; this pass did not re-read them at line, and no cell in this block is
offered as verified-by-me unless it says `re-read here`. Cells that are this appendix's own wording are marked
`[paraphrase — not the filing's wording]`.** `[src: …]` keys are narrative-row and dossier keys, not register ids
(RD-078: the ~219 collided Stage-3 register rows cannot be joined by id, so every locator below is document+line).

F. CUSTOMER (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

**Standing reading limit for §F (F42–F78): the `Passage:` cells below are the strings the narrative prints with
their `sources/` line anchors, transcribed; this pass did not re-read them at line, and no cell here is offered
as verified by me unless it says `re-read here`. `[paraphrase — not the filing's wording]` marks this

G63 Claim: The status test applied per site — **a lease signature is not an opening; a press release is not an opening; an equipment contract is not an opening; a capacity figure in a shareholder letter is not an opening** — status is taken from the filing's own verb — Date: method — Source: `stage_3_part_1.md` §G.1 — Source date: n/a — Tier: 1 — Class: METHOD — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: n/a — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: None; this is the test that makes the nine-opening count defensible. [src: s3p1 §G.1 test]

G64 Claim: Nine openings are dated inside the window on the filings' own verbs (G2 in 1997; G3–G8 and G11 in 1999; G9/G10 running from 1998-10), with **five of them in a single nine-month period and no opening day for any**; the FY1999 10-K closes the estate at "a total of 10 distribution centers" — Date: 1999-12-31 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.579-580, l.1596-1604, l.1610-1612; `sources/10-Q_Q3-1999_…` l.830-837 — Source date: 2000-03-23; 1999-11-15 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED (count) — Passage: "We now have a total of 10 distribution centers, including our facilities in Seattle, Washington, and New Castle, Delaware"; "eight distribution centers located in Seattle, Washington; New Castle, Delaware; … These distribution centers comprise a total of approximately 3.8 million square feet"; "a distribution center in each country with a combined 690,000 square feet" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None on the count: **"five states opened" (Q3-1999) reconciles to "six US sites" (FY1999) because Kentucky contributes two cities**, Campbellsville and Lexington, so there is no count conflict; the residual defect is the square-footage pairing at G65. [src: s3p1 §G.1 count]

G65 Claim: **The two different "eights"** — "these eight new distribution centers comprised approximately four million square feet" (six US + two European **openings**) against "eight distribution centers … approximately 3.8 million square feet" (the **US estate** including two pre-existing sites): the word counts different sets and **must not be summed** — Date: 1999-12-31 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.572-574 vs l.1602-1605 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (aggregation trap) — Passage: "these eight new distribution centers comprised approximately four million square feet" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (one document, two captions; the trap is internal to the filing) — Conflicts: **M-14** → §U family. 3.8M US + 690K European is the endpoint estate; ~4M is the 1999 increment. [src: s3p1 §G.1 G13]

G66 Claim: The headline expansion multiple is **15.7×** in square footage, 285,000 sq ft at 1997-12-31 to 4,490,000 sq ft at 1999-12-31 — and it is footage, not throughput — Date: 1997-12-31 → 1999-12-31 — Source: FY1997 Item 2; FY1999 10-K l.1596-1612 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DERIVED — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High (arithmetic); n/a as capacity — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. **Arithmetic:** 85,000 + 200,000 = 285,000; 3,800,000 + 690,000 = 4,490,000; 4,490,000 ÷ 285,000 = 15.75×. It includes sites opened weeks before the count date, and $83,290K of the assets behind it was not in service (G72). [src: s3p1 §G.1 G14]

G67 Claim: Endpoint sensitivity for the estate — under a **1999-06-30** close the estate is five sites of which four were operating a year earlier (Nevada the only new automated site), and the entire G3–G8 build-out, the ten-site total and §G.3's 1999 cost step are post-boundary: **the single largest content difference between the contested endpoints** — Date: 1999-06-30 vs 1999-12-31 — Source: `stage_3_part_1.md` §G.1 endpoint note — Source date: n/a — Tier: 1 — Class: METHOD (sensitivity) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: **U.153** (the boundary contest). [src: s3p1 §G.1 ⚑]

G68 Claim: The five Buschman exhibits of 1999-03-11 are **materials-handling equipment purchase contracts with Amazon as buyer**; the intake manifest's "marketplace contract" label is refuted by the instrument, and the correction is recorded not silently applied — Date: 1999-03-11 — Source: `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_acc-0000891020-99-000894_filed-1999-05-17.txt` Exhibits 10.1–10.5, EX-10.1 l.1783-1798 — Source date: 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (instrument identity) — Passage: "This Sales Agreement, hereinafter called "Sales Agreement," made by and between Amazon.com, Inc., a Delaware corporation (hereinafter called "Purchaser"), and The Buschman Company, an Ohio corporation (hereinafter called "Seller"), constitutes the agreement of the parties as follows:" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **M-15** → §U family: five exhibits, one accession, identical standard terms — **five printings of one instrument family, not five sources**. [src: s3p1 §G.2]

G69 Claim: Exhibit 10.1 is a **Phase I Fernley, Nevada** proposal dated 1999-01-18 — equipment specified six weeks before the contract and while the building was still "expected to begin operations in 1999": the machine estate has its own chronology, independent of the lease chronology — Date: 1999-01-18 (proposal) / 1999-03-11 (contract) — Source: EX-10.1 l.1810-1811 — Source date: 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "Phase I Fernley, Nevada Proposal for Amazon.com, Inc." — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. G70 Claim: Exhibit 10.2 is a **Phase II Fernley** proposal dated 1999-02-05 — a second expansion of the same site contracted in the same filing set, before Phase I equipment was accepted — Date: 1999-02-05 — Source: EX-10.2 l.3520 — Source date: 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "Phase II Fernley, Nevada" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-15 (one instrument family). G71 Claim: Exhibits 10.3/10.4/10.5 are **three contracts for sites the company could not yet name** — "Site A", "Site B", "Site C" "yet to be determined" under one 1999-02-05 standard proposal and one price structure: the strongest document-level proof in the corpus that the 1999 network was a programme in progress rather than a built estate — Date: 1999-03-11 (contracts) / 1999-02-05 (proposal) — Source: EX-10.3 l.5470-5476; EX-10.4 l.7313-7319; EX-10.5 l.9172-9178 — Source date: 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "The Standard Proposal for Amazon.com, Inc. dated February 5, 1999 … such proposal shall apply to "Site A" yet to be determined" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (three exhibits, one accession, one instrument family: §3 counts one) — Conflicts: M-15. G72 Claim: The only machine description in the window specific enough to read as engineering is an **accepted option** on EX-10.2's OPTIONS page, and the word "existing" inside an option to the Phase II proposal shows Phase I equipment was installed or committed to configuration by 1999-03-11 — Date: 1999-03-11 — Source: EX-10.2 l.3443-3458 — Source date: 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + INFERENCE — Passage: "Add gravity flanker conveyor to second side of existing powered take-away on the third level of the 8 existing pick module fingers." marked "[X] ACCEPTED" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. [src: s3p1 §G.2 G18]

G73 Claim: Installation was by the **Seller's non-union crews** with overtime reserved for tie-ins affecting operations, against a **blanket order** — equipment manufactured and warehoused before sites were chosen: the procurement shape of a build-out running ahead of site selection — Date: 1999-03-11 — Source: EX-10.2 l.5400-5402, l.5406-5408 — Source date: 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "Installation services are based on using nonunion labor during regular working hours. The equipment being manufactured and stored, based on the blanket order…" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. Labour identity question raised here is answered at G81. G76 Claim: **No labour identity** — the installation crews were the Seller's and nothing in the corpus says who ran the automation afterwards — Date: 1999 — Source: absence across `sources/` — Source date: n/a — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: None. G77 Claim: **Fulfilment costs are filed for the whole window in one note, for the first time anywhere:** $12.1M (1997), $50.3M (1998), $188.4M (1999) — Date: FY1997/FY1998/FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_acc-0000891020-00-000622_filed-2000-03-23.txt` l.3097-3103 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited, `(PB)` document carrying in-window states) — Passage: "Fulfillment costs amounted to $188.4 million, $50.3 million and $12.1 million in 1999, 1998 and 1997, respectively." — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **M-16** → §U family (the FY1998 report prints 1998 marketing-and-sales without a fulfilment split). Fulfilment is defined to include distribution- and customer-service staffing, receiving, inspecting, warehousing, picking, packaging and answering customer inquiries. [src: s3p1 §G.3]

G78 Claim: Fulfilment cost as a share of net sales is **8.19% (1997), 8.25% (1998), 11.49% (1999)** on the FY1999 restated basis — the build did not reduce fulfilment cost per sales dollar: flat for two years, then +3.2 points in one — Date: 1997 → 1999 — Source: fulfilment l.3101-3103; net sales l.1732 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. **Arithmetic:** 12,100 ÷ 147,787 = 0.08188; 50,300 ÷ 609,819 = 0.08248; 188,400 ÷ 1,639,839 = 0.11489. Denominators are the **restated** FY1999-basis sales ($147,787K not the as-filed $147,758K); using as-filed 1997 sales changes the ratio only in the fourth decimal, and the choice is stated rather than hidden. [src: s3p1 §G.3 G20–G22]

G79 Claim: **1999 is the first year in the company's life in which it spent more on running the network than on buying demand** — fulfilment ÷ advertising = 0.571 (1997), 0.835 (1998), 1.337 (1999) — a fact about priority, not efficiency — Date: 1997 → 1999 — Source: l.3101-3103, l.3108-3109 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. **Arithmetic:** 12.1 ÷ 21.2 = 0.5708; 50.3 ÷ 60.2 = 0.8355; 188.4 ÷ 140.9 = 1.3371. [src: s3p1 §G.3]

G80 Claim: Fulfilment grew **15.6×** in two years against net sales **11.1×** — the cost line outran the revenue line — and the table does **not** permit the inference that the network was uneconomic, which requires a counterfactual the record does not hold — Date: 1997 → 1999 — Source: l.3101-3103, l.1732 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DERIVED + INFERENCE LIMIT — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High (arithmetic) — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. **Arithmetic:** 188.4 ÷ 12.1 = 15.57×; 1,639,839 ÷ 147,787 = 11.10×. The company's own forward statement is carried as an expectation, not a result: "fulfillment costs will decline as a percentage of sales in the future as additional capacity of our existing distribution center network is more fully utilized" (l.1988-1990). [src: s3p1 §G.3]

G81 Claim: Advertising's falling share of net sales (21.6% → 14.3% → 9.9% → 8.60% across 1996–99) is a **decomposition effect inside a composite line**: the company's own marketing-and-sales ÷ net sales runs 27.1% (1997) → 21.8% (1998) → **25.2% (1999)**, so the composite rose 3.4 points in the same year advertising's share fell 1.3 — Date: 1996 → 1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.1960, l.1737 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DERIVED (§14.8 hazard) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-16. Fulfilment's share of the combined caption: 30.2% / 37.9% / 45.6%, from $40,077K / $132,654K / $413,150K (l.1737). [src: s3p1 §G.3]

G82 Claim: **Capex (purchases of fixed assets) ran $7,603K (1997), $28,333K (1998), $287,055K (1999)** — FY1999 alone is 8.0× the two prior years combined — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` statement of cash flows l.2852 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. **Arithmetic:** 28,333 ÷ 7,603 = 3.73×; 287,055 ÷ 28,333 = 10.13×; 287,055 ÷ 35,936 = 7.99×. [src: s3p1 §G.4 G23]

G83 Claim: **Gross fixed assets print two different values for the same year** — FY1997 appears as 12,899 in the FY1997 10-K and 13,490 in the FY1998 10-K's 1997 column (Δ +591 gross, +461 net, every line higher) with **no reconciling note anywhere in the corpus** — Date: FY1997 / FY1998 / FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1997_…` l.2300-2308; `sources/10-K_FY1998_…` l.2745-2753; `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.3493 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (restatement pair) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] 12,899 / 13,490 / 43,585 / 366,977 — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (three annual reports of one issuer re-witnessing one asset line; a version difference is conflict, not corroboration) — Conflicts: **M-17** → §U family. Rule applied: use the FY1999 restated series for any cross-year curve and print the as-filed value beside it; **never average**. 366,977 ÷ 43,585 = 8.42× in one year. [src: s3p1 §G.4 G24]

G84 Claim: **Construction in progress was $83,290K at 1999-12-31 = 22.7% of gross fixed assets** — more than a fifth of the estate not yet in service, and therefore not yet depreciating, at the count date — Date: 1999-12-31 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.3491 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **M-13** (the build-out reading). **Arithmetic:** 83,290 ÷ 366,977 = 0.2269. Prior year $1,760K. What it does **not** show: no filing gives a completion date for any site, so it is an accounting state and not a schedule. [src: s3p1 §G.4 G25]

G85 Claim: **Leased assets went from $442K to $52,374K (118.5×)** — the new machine estate is being financed, not bought — Date: 1998 → 1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.3490 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. [src: s3p1 §G.4 G26]

G86 Claim: The **first capitalised-interest figure in the corpus** is "$3.4 million … during the year ended December 31, 1999"; none is filed for 1997 or 1998 — Date: 1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.3500-3501 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (+ DOCUMENTED ABSENCE for 1997/98) — Passage: "$3.4 million … during the year ended December 31, 1999" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. [src: s3p1 §G.4 G27]

G87 Claim: **Depreciation and amortisation has no single filed value for 1997/1998** — 3,388 (own 10-K) against 3,442 (restated) for 1997, and 9,692 against 9,421 for 1998, across three annual reports; FY1999 is 36,806 — Date: FY1997 / FY1998 / FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1997_…` l.2037; `sources/10-K_FY1998_…` l.2232; `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.2820 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (restatement pairs, both witnesses named) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] 3,388 / 3,442 / 9,692 / 9,421 / 36,806 — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **M-17** and **U.158** (no single filed value for 1997/98 technology spend or depreciation). **Arithmetic:** single 1999 quarters from 10-Q cumulatives (l.324/l.299/l.314 minus FY total): Q1 5,223 · Q2 8,102 · Q3 9,610 · Q4 13,871, and **Q4-1999 alone exceeds all of FY1998** on the restated basis (13,871 ÷ 9,421 = 1.47×). Never average the pairs. [src: s3p1 §G.4 G28]

G88 Claim: **Merchandise inventories went $8,971K (1997) → $29,501K (1998) → $220,646K (1999)**, the single largest working-capital movement in the window — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1998_…` l.1957; `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.2545 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: None. **Arithmetic:** ×3.29 then ×7.48; 24.6× over two years. J50 Claim: **There is no single filed value for 1996/1997/1998 technology spend:** the same line is printed three different ways for the same year under two captions — 1996: 2,313 (FY1997 10-K, "Product development") / 2,401 (FY1998 comparative, "Technology and content") / 2,377 (FY1999 Selected Data); 1997: **12,485 / 13,916 / 13,384**; 1998: **46,807 / 46,424**; 1999: 159,722 — Date: FY1996–FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1997_…` l.1183, l.1463, l.1867; `sources/10-K_FY1998_…` comparative; `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` l.1738, l.2015, l.2614 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (all witnesses printed, none averaged) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (three annual reports of one issuer re-witnessing one line: a version difference, which is conflict, not corroboration) — Conflicts: **M-24 → U.158**. **Rule: any technology-spend curve assembled across filings is wrong unless it names its source document at every point;** the re-caption is not cosmetic, and any derived ratio moves by up to $1,431K per year (11%) on the choice of document. K. MONEY / FINANCIAL FORENSICS (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

**Standing reading limit for §K: `Passage:` cells are the strings the narrative and `ST3_B_finance.md` print
with their `sources/` line anchors (capital `L` numbering as the dossiers print it), transcribed; this pass did
not re-read them at line unless a cell says `re-read here`. `[paraphrase — not the filing's wording]` marks this
appendix's own wording. `UNKNOWN` stands where the dossier recorded it and no value is proxied.**
**Basis discipline (method §6, §8), carried forward from Stage 2 §K and §P:** "Net sales" is the audited revenue
line on a **shipment** basis, inclusive of outbound shipping-and-handling charged to customers and, from FY1998,
of commissions earned on third-party developer sales; it is never GMV, never bookings, never orders-received.
FY = calendar year ending 31 December; USD thousands unless stated. **Money inside one registration lineage is
one source.** Every ratio whose denominator was never filed is printed UNKNOWN.

K35 Claim: The Stage-3 money record has **four witnesses to the same fiscal years**, and the section's geometry keeps them apart: (i) FY1997 `10-K405` (1998-03-30) for FY1997/1996/1995 **as filed**; (ii) the FY1998 Form 10-K (acc. 0000891020-99-000375, 1999-03-05) for FY1998 contemporaneous and the earlier years **restated**; (iii) the Q3-1999 `10-Q` (1999-11-15) for nine-month 1999; (iv) the **FY1999 Form 10-K** (acc. 0000891020-00-000622, 2000-03-23) **with its 10-K/A** (acc. 0000891020-00-001638, 2000-09-08), which restates the earlier years a second time and re-cuts the expense captions — Date: 1998-03-30 → 2000-09-08 — Source: as listed in `sources/` — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: METHOD (witness geometry) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: **1 for (iv) taken whole: a 10-K and its own 10-K/A are one document restated, not two witnesses** — Conflicts: U.154, U.157, U.159. Two auditors' reports sit inside the window: the FY1998 opinion is dated **1999-01-22** ("except for Note 11, as to which the date is February 10, 1999"), covering each of the three years ended 31 December 1998 (FY1998 10-K L1931-1935). [src: s3p2 §K.1 preamble; ST3B]

K36 Claim: **The audited annual series as filed each year:** net sales 147,758 (1997) / 609,996 (1998); gross profit 28,813 / 133,841 = margin **19.5% / 21.9%**; loss from operations (29,209) / (111,960); net loss (27,590) / (124,546); interest income 1,898 / 14,053; interest expense (279) / (26,639); accumulated deficit (33,615) / (162,060); stockholders' equity 28,486 / 138,745; total assets 149,006 / 648,460 — and the shape that matters is that gross margin **fell** 22.0% (1996) → 19.5% (1997) then **recovered** to 21.9% (1998), breaking Stage 2's three-year decline at the first Stage-3 year — Date: FY1997 / FY1998 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1997_acc-0000891020-98-000448_filed-1998-03-30.txt` L1177-1197, L1861-1881; `sources/10-K_FY1998_acc-0000891020-99-000375_filed-1999-03-05.txt` L1222-1247, L2016-2039 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited) + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (each year's own annual report is one witness to its own year; the recovery is one document's line) — Conflicts: the recovery is partly the pooling recast (K38) and partly mix. **Arithmetic:** 609,996 ÷ 147,758 = 4.128 → filed growth **+312.8%**, the third consecutive year of triple-digit growth on a base 4× the previous one. [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 1; ST3B §S1-§S2]

K37 Claim: **The same years as re-printed in the FY1999 10-K — a second restatement and a caption re-cut:** FY1999/FY1998/FY1997 net sales **1,639,839 / 609,819 / 147,787**; gross profit 290,645 / 133,664 / 28,818; marketing and sales 413,150 / 132,654 / 40,077; **technology and content 159,722 / 46,424 / 13,384 (relabelled from "product development")**; G&A 70,144 / 15,618 / 6,741; stock-based compensation 30,618 / 1,889 / 1,211; **amortization of goodwill and other intangibles 214,694 / 42,599 / —**; merger/acquisition/investment-related costs 8,072 / 3,535 / —; loss from operations (605,755) / (109,055) / (32,595); interest expense (84,566) / (26,639) / (326); **equity in losses of equity-method investees (76,769) / (2,905) / —**; **net loss (719,968) / (124,546) / (31,020)**; LPS (2.20) / (0.42) / (0.12) on 326,753 / 296,344 / 260,682 thousand shares — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1999_acc-0000891020-00-000622_filed-2000-03-23.txt` L2608-2646 (income statement), L1732-1761 (Selected Financial Data), L1842 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited FY1999) + FACT (restated) + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (same lineage as the FY1999 10-K/A; the 10-K re-witnessing 1997/1998 is not a second measurement) — Conflicts: **U.159**. **Arithmetic:** 290,645 ÷ 1,639,839 = **17.7%** FY1999 margin; 1,639,839 ÷ 609,819 = 2.689 → **+168.9%**, the company printing "169%" itself at L1842 — the first year in the project in which the growth **rate** falls below 300%. [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 2]

K39 Claim: **Line-by-line FY1997 restatement deltas (RESTATED − AS-FILED, $000):** net sales 147,758 → 147,787 (**+29**); cost of sales 118,945 → 118,969 (+24); gross profit 28,813 → 28,818 (+5); marketing and sales 38,964 → 40,486 (**+1,522**); product development 12,485 → 13,916 (**+1,431**); G&A 6,573 → 7,011 (+438); total operating expenses 58,022 → 61,413 (+3,391); loss from operations (29,209) → (32,595) (**(3,386)**); interest income 1,898 → 1,901 (+3); interest expense (279) → (326) ((47)); net loss (27,590) → (31,020) (**(3,430)**); accumulated deficit (33,615) → (37,514) (**(3,899)**); accounts payable 32,697 → 33,027 (+330); total assets 149,006 → 149,844 (+838); working capital 93,517 → 93,158 (**(359)**); stockholders' equity 28,486 → 28,591 (+105) — Date: FY1997 — Source: 10-K405 FY1997 vs 10-K FY1998 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (pairs) + DERIVED (deltas) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: U.126 family. **Arithmetic:** 3,386 ÷ 29,209 = 11.6% — 1997's operating loss grew 11.6% purely by restatement; 3,430 ÷ 27,590 = **12.4%** on the net loss; PlanetAll added $29k of 1997 sales, "not significant" per FY1999 10-K L3362 ("Net sales for PlanetAll were not significant and net loss was $4.1 million"); the deficit moved (3,899), more than the year's own delta, so earlier years moved too; **working capital is the only direction that argues against the company.** [src: s3p2 §K.2 table]

K43 Claim: **FY1998's +$31,035k of operating cash is not trading cash.** The audited reconciliation ($000): net loss (124,546) + D&A 9,692 + amortization of deferred stock-based compensation 2,386 + **non-cash merger/acquisition costs 47,065** + **non-cash interest 23,970** = **net cash used before changes in operating assets and liabilities (41,433)**; then accounts payable **+78,674**, other liabilities and accrued expenses +21,448 (printed +21,615 in the FY1999 restatement), accrued advertising +9,617, less inventories (20,513), prepaid (16,465), deposits (293) = **+72,468**; total **+31,035** — Date: FY1998 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L2229-2270; 10-K FY1999 L2817-2848, L2257-2269 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited cash-flow lines) + DERIVED — Passage: "was primarily attributable to non-cash expenses and increases in accounts payable and accrued expenses, largely offset by the net loss and increases in inventories and prepaid expenses and other" (FY1999 10-K L2266-2269 describing 1998; the FY1998 10-K's own sentence is identical in substance, L1621-1625) — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.128** family. **Arithmetic:** the three float lines sum to +109,739 = **3.5×** the reported positive figure (31,035 × 3.5 = 108,623); payables alone are **2.53×** it (78,674 ÷ 31,035); −124,546 + 9,692 + 2,386 + 47,065 + 23,970 = −41,433 ✓. K45 Claim: **FY1999 operating cash flow was $(90,875)k** — printed both as the statement line (L2848) and in the MD&A as "Net cash used by operating activities amounted to $90.9 million for 1999", "primarily attributable to the net loss for the year of $720 million, partially offset by non-cash charges … totaling $358.9 million, as well as $230.1 million of cash provided by changes in operating assets and liabilities" — **supplier float grew to +$330,166k and the year still consumed cash** — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L2257-2269, L2561, L2837, L2848 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (statement line and MD&A are one document) — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** payables inflow +78,674 (1998) → +330,166 (1999) = 4.2×; accounts payable balance 33,027 → 113,273 → 463,026; **DPO = AP ÷ cost of sales × 365 = 101.3 → 86.8 → 125.2 days**, i.e. terms **tightened** ~15 days in 1998 and stretched ~38 days in 1999. [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 5, §K.3 item 3]

K46 Claim: **The "first positive operating cash flow" claim is removed from the validation register as a signal at any date** — both halves of "1998 was the first year Amazon generated positive operating cash flow" are wrong, and the corrections run in opposite directions: 1997 was already positive (+3,522 filed / +687 restated) and 1998's figure is not trading cash — Date: FY1997–FY1998 — Source: K42–K45 — Source date: as above — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + RETRACTION — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] FY1998's $31,035k is the year the figure becomes *material*, not the year it becomes *positive* — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: registered (§K.3 `CONFLICT FOR §U`, ST3E-21 / OC-205); **§L does not carry it as a signal.** [src: s3p2 §K.3 items 1–2]

K47 Claim: **What a manager could legitimately have concluded in March 1999** on filed evidence: that at $610m of sales the business consumed less cash than its loss implied, and did so through a payables relationship with its distributors. **What no one could conclude from it:** that any order contributed positively — Date: 1999-03-05 — Source: FY1998 10-K cash-flow statement and MD&A — Source date: 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE (bounded) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. Alternative on the record: ordinary retail float at inventory-led growth — but inventory consumed cash in both years ((20,513) then (172,069)) and no vendor contract guaranteeing terms exists in any filing in the window, the position Stage 2 recorded for Ingram at 58% of purchases. [src: s3p2 §K.3 item 4]

K48 Claim: **Capital expenditure ran $1,335k (FY1996) → $7,603k (FY1997) → $28,333k (FY1998) → $181,859k (9M-1999) → $287,055k (FY1999)**; capex ÷ net sales 4.6% (1998) → 17.5% (1999), so capex is where the 1999 money went and grew faster than sales by ~3.8×; gross fixed assets $43,585k → $366,977k (8.4×) of which construction in progress $1,760k → $83,290k (**22.7%** of the gross estate not yet in service, not yet depreciating); leased assets $442k → $52,374k (118.5×) — Date: FY1996–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L2251, Item 2; 10-K FY1999 L2852, L3487-3501, L573-574, L1604-1605 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "The Company does not own any real estate." (both years' Item 2) — Conf: High; the sq-ft comparisons are DERIVED sums of filed components — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. Filed estate: 285,000 sq ft at 1997-12-31 (85,000 Seattle + 200,000 New Castle) → ~3.8 million sq ft in eight US DCs; **of the 616,000 sq ft filed at 1998-12-31, 323,000 sq ft (Fernley, leased December 1998, expiring 2009) was leased but not yet operating — over half the estate.** First capitalised-interest figure in the corpus: the Company "capitalized approximately $3.4 million of interest during the year ended December 31, 1999". [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 6; ST3D-30; ST3B-21]

K49 Claim: **The FY1998 interest line is ~90% accretion, not cash — the single most mis-readable number in Stage 3:** interest expense (326) → (26,639) in a year, **but $23,970k of the 1998 charge is non-cash**, so the cash cost was ≈$2,669k; FY1999 shows (84,566) with 29,171 non-cash add-back — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L1519-1521, L1955-1996, L2229-2270, L2238; 10-K FY1999 L2860 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 26,639 − 23,970 = 2,669. Debt: long-term $76,521k (FY1997 **as filed**) / $76,702k (**restated**) → $348,077k + $684k current = $348,761k (FY1998); Item 6 prints "Long-term debt 348,140" and the S-3 of 1999-03-16 recites "$349 million of outstanding senior indebtedness" — **a $621k presentation difference inside one document, recorded not reconciled**. Cash + marketable securities $125,066k (1997) → $373,445k (1998) → $706.2m (1999). [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 7]

K50 Claim: **Founder pay, and the inversion the proxy table prints:** Jeffrey P. Bezos, President and CEO, salary $64,333 (1996) / $79,197 (1997) / $81,840 (1998), **$0 bonus in all three years**, **no securities underlying options granted in any of the three years**, "all other compensation" nil; same 1998 table: Dalzell $201,512, Aposporos $142,083, Spiegel $116,352, Risher $105,168 — the founder was paid 41% of the highest-paid VP shown and **less than four of his own direct reports** — Date: 1996–1998 (three-year table) — Source: `sources/DEF14A_1999-proxy-statement_acc-0000891020-99-000635_filed-1999-04-07.txt`; `sources/DEF14A_1998-proxy-statement_acc-0000891020-98-000601_filed-1998-04-17.txt`; FY1998 10-K Item 13 → proxy; L1989 — Source date: 1999-04-07; 1998-04-17; 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED + documented null — Passage: "Note receivable from officer for common stock … **(1,099)**" [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High; UNKNOWN (the officer-note borrower) — Corroboration: 1 (proxy and 10-K Item 13 are one disclosure set for one year) — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 81,840 ÷ 201,512 = 0.406; 81,840 ÷ 64,333 = +27.2% across two years against net sales ×38.7 (15,746 → 609,996 = 3,770%). Related-party items complete as filed: Cook's and Stonesifer's 2,500 shares each at $40.00 (Series A); a **$75,000 interest-free relocation loan to Dalzell, "repaid on October 23, 1998"**; and the $1,099k officer note, **borrower unnamed and not to be equated with the Dalzell loan**. His cash pay moved while his position did not: the mechanism the filings supply is equity already held, with no 1996-98 grants, so it moved only through the three splits (K79). [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 8; ST3B §K1-§K2]

K51 Claim: **Third-party commissions sit inside the revenue line and the definition of "net sales" therefore changed inside the stage:** from Q2-1999 net sales "include auctions revenue, which is comprised of placement fees and sales commissions on closed auctions"; from Q3-1999 and in the FY1999 10-K, "Net sales also include commissions from auctions and zShops transactions, which include sales commissions, placement fees and fees from payment service transactions" — Date: 1999-08-16 → 2000-03-23 — Source: `sources/10-Q_Q2-1999_…` L853-854; `sources/10-Q_Q3-1999_…` L863-865; 10-K FY1999 L1845-1850 — Source date: 1999-08-16; 1999-11-15; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (basis) + NEGATIVE RESULT (take rate) — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.160**. **Q3-1999's $355,777k and Q2-1997's $27,855k are not the same kind of number, and a take rate is structurally unknowable** (K61). [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 9; ST3B §U1]

K52 Claim: **§14.11 fill — shipping revenue, a Stage-2 UNKNOWN, is now filed:** "Shipping revenue was $239 million, $94.1 million and $24.8 million in 1999, 1998 and 1997, respectively", stated in the same paragraph confirming net sales "also include outbound shipping charges" — the **first** filing in the project to quantify the postage leg of revenue — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1845-1850, L1847-1848, L1896-1897 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited MD&A); UNKNOWN (shipping margin) — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High (amount, annual basis) — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none; the stale Stage-2 null (#7) is **not reproduced**. **Arithmetic:** share of net sales 24.8 ÷ 147.787 = 16.8% (1997); 94.1 ÷ 609.8 = 15.4% (1998); 239 ÷ 1,639.8 = 14.6% (1999). Printed to three significant figures **annually only**, so no quarterly version may be inferred; the cost side sits inside cost of sales unsplit, so shipping margin remains uncomputable and the MD&A's "we realized lower shipping margins in 1999 due to an increase in partial shipments" asserts a direction, not a number. [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 10]

K53 Claim: **Q4-1999 inventory charges, as the company quantifies its own worst quarter: approximately $39 million**, in the MD&A and repeated in the risk factor, with the causal chain filed as a disclosed risk that then materialised in the same page — Date: 1999 Q4 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1043-1050, L1890-1892, L598-601 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (company statement against interest) + DERIVED — Passage: "We may fail to accurately predict the optimal inventory levels at our distribution centers for the fourth quarter. … If we overstock products, we may be required to take significant inventory mark-downs or write-offs, which could reduce gross margins"; then "In the fourth quarter of 1999, we incurred inventory-related charges, which significantly decreased our gross margins" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (MD&A and risk factor are one document) — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 39,000 ÷ 1,639,839 = 2.4% of net sales; 39,000 ÷ 290,645 = **13.4% of the year's entire gross profit** — a single quarter's inventory misjudgement consumed one seventh of the year's margin, in the year the network was built. (G101's "20.7% of fulfilment cost" is the same $39m over $188.4m; both denominators are printed so neither reading is forced.) **No causal link and no amount is stated in the risk factor itself** (L598-601). [src: s3p2 §K.1 row 11]

K54 Claim: **The $1,250,000,000 4¾% convertible subordinated notes due 2009 were announced at $500 million and priced the same day at approximately $1.25 billion** — the deal was 2.5× its own announced size before any book was public — and completed 1999-02-03, with Q1-1999 cash flow printing "Proceeds from long-term debt 1,250,000" — Date: 1999-01-28 (announce/price) / 1999-02-03 (close) — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1999-01-28_acc-0000891020-99-000107_filed-1999-01-28.txt` and `sources/8-K_event-1999-01-28_acc-0000891020-99-000110_filed-1999-01-29.txt`; `sources/8-K_event-1999-02-03_acc-0000891020-99-000125_filed-1999-02-04.txt`; 10-Q Q1-1999 Note 2; 10-K FY1998 L1761-1766 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "Proceeds from long-term debt 1,250,000" [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (**two 8-K accessions for one event on consecutive days are one instrument's paper, not two witnesses**) — Conflicts: none. [src: s3p2 §K.4 row 1]

EX-4.1); `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_…` L1536-1560; 10-K FY1998 L1724, L3224 — Source date: 1999-02-04; 1999-05-17; 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (executed instrument) — Passage: "may be converted into, in the aggregate, 8,009,996 shares" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none; the exhibits are part of the filing that carries them (one filing, per §3). K56 Claim: **Conversion price on the 12× split vintage:** $156.055 is the price at 1999-02-03, i.e. after the 2-for-1 of 1998-06-01 and the 3-for-1 of 1999-01-04 but **before the 2-for-1 paid 1999-09-01**; the indenture's adjustment clause covers "the issuance of common stock as a dividend" and "subdivisions", and the FY1999 10-K states the split happened — Date: 1999-02-03 → 1999-09-01 — Source: 10-Q Q1-1999 L1546-1551; 10-K FY1999 L1692, L4987; 8-K event 1999-10-28 L521 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "the 2-for-1 stock split effected September 1, 1999" — Conf: High that the clause and the split are filed; the restated price is DERIVED — Corroboration: 1 (all four carriers are one corporate lineage) — Conflicts: **U.157**. **Arithmetic:** conversion price **$78.0275**, conversion shares **16,019,992** (1,250,000,000 ÷ 78.0275 = 16,019,993). **Any per-share figure in this window inherited at 6× must be halved to reach the final vintage.** [src: s3p2 §K.4 row 3]

K57 Claim: Use of proceeds, as the registrant stated it, names **working capital to fund anticipated operating losses** first among uses — Date: 1999-02 → 1999-03-05 (filing) — Source: 10-K FY1998 L1761-1766 — Source date: 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: COMPANY STATE (issuer statement, contemporaneous) — Passage: "general corporate purposes, including working capital to fund anticipated operating losses, the expansion of the Company's core business, investments in new business segments and markets, capital expenditures, acquisitions or investments in complementary businesses, products and technologies and repurchases and retirement of debt" — Conf: High that filed — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. A stated intention, not an allocation record. [src: s3p2 §K.4 row 4]

K58 Claim: **What the converts displaced:** in Q1-1999 the Company repurchased **$126.0 million face amount** of its own 10% Senior Discount Notes, "representing accreted value of $83.9 million", leaving $404 million of the $530 million principal outstanding; FY1999 prints $178.4 million of cash paid to repurchase them — **the only place in the window where the company retires its own debt at a discount**, and it changed the *character* of the cash obligation from no cash interest before 2003-11-01 to **4¾% cash from 1999-08-01** — Date: 1999 Q1 → 1999-12-31 — Source: 10-Q Q1-1999 L436-442; 10-K FY1999 L2279-2283, L2832 — Source date: 1999-05-17; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "representing accreted value of $83.9 million" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 0.0475 × 1,250,000,000 = **$59.4m per year** of cash interest against a business whose trading generation was **−$320,987k**. [src: s3p2 §K.4 row 5]

K59 Claim: **The two-step leverage pivot, sized:** 1997-11-07/December 1997 — $75,000,000 three-year **senior secured** term facility (Deutsche Bank AG administrative agent, Deutsche Morgan Grenfell arranger), first-priority lien on substantially all assets, LIBOR + 3.50% then 4.00%, covenants on minimum cash, EBITDA, capex **and the accounts-payable aging**, 50% sweep of any future raise, dividends prohibited, 750,000 warrants at $52.11; 1998-04-24/05 — $75.0m of it retired with proceeds of **10% Senior Discount Notes due 2008**, announced $275m, upsized 1998-05-05 to ~$326m gross / ~$315.7m net, sold at a discount to a **$530m principal at maturity**, no cash interest before 2003-11-01, change-of-control put at 101% of accreted value; 1999-02-03 — $1.25bn converts — Date: 1997-11-07 → 1999-02-03 — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1997-11-07_acc-0000950151-97-000357_filed-1997-11-10.txt`; `sources/8-K_event-1998-04-24_acc-0000891020-98-000653_filed-1998-04-27.txt`; `sources/8-K_event-1998-05-05_acc-0000891020-98-000722_filed-1998-05-06.txt`; `sources/424B2_final-prospectus_acc-0000891020-98-001279_filed-1998-08-13.txt` L1345-1360; `sources/10-Q_Q1-1998_…` EX-4.1/4.2/4.3; 10-K FY1998 L1519-1527, L2256-2264, L1638-1639 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] the decision was to trade a covenant-bearing secured bank loan that **capped the payables lever** for unsecured paper whose cash cost was deferred — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (one financing sequence in the issuer's own paper; each instrument is separately evidenced but the sequence is not three witnesses) — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** FY1998 financing section 325,987 + 8,383 + 5,983 − 78,108 − 7,783 = **254,462**, foots exactly. The $2.0m of unamortised loan fees written off on prepayment (L1526-1527) is the filed price of doing it early. [src: s3p2 §K.4 row 6]

K60 Claim: **Non-cash equity is the larger number:** "Common stock issued in connection with acquisitions $217,241" (1998) and **$774,409** (1999), nil in 1997 — FY1998's stock consideration was **67% of that year's entire debt raise** and FY1999's **61% of the converts** and **2.7×** the year's capex: the cash raised by selling securities into the market ($326.0m + $1.25bn = $1.576bn gross across the window) is **not** the bigger financing instrument in this stage — paper handed to targets is — Date: FY1998 / FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L2273-2277; 10-K FY1999 L2875 (supplemental non-cash disclosure) — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "Common stock issued in connection with acquisitions $217,241" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none; the valuation reference price/date is undisclosed, so this is a filed non-cash amount and not a price (cf. E78). **Arithmetic:** 217,241 ÷ 325,987 = 0.666; 774,409 ÷ 1,250,000 = 0.620; 774,409 ÷ 287,055 = 2.70. [src: s3p2 §K.4 row 7]

What would have been needed — a segment or line disaggregating marketplace revenue from merchandise revenue — **exists nowhere and was never proposed in any filing** — Date: 1999 → 1999-12-31 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1845-1850; segment note L4155-4162 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE (structural) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: **U.160**.
