K70 Claim: **Cost of sales was defined by three named components for the first time — "the cost of merchandise sold to customers, as well as inbound and outbound shipping costs and the cost of tangible supplies used to package product for shipment" — named and still not quantified separately**; merchandise-only COGS, packaging cost and shipping margin therefore stay UNKNOWN; payment-processing/merchant fees are now **named twice** as an expense driver ("credit card fees" inside marketing and sales, "increased credit card fees resulting from higher sales") and quantified nowhere; **no total payroll or total-compensation figure is filed in any year**, though stock-based compensation became its own line (1,211 / 1,889 / 30,618) and goodwill amortisation a separate one (— / 42,599 / 214,694) — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1886-1889, L1964, L1975, L2616 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE (amounts) + FACT (components, captions) — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. [src: s3p2 §K.6 rows 6, 8–10]

K71 Claim: **Return amount / return rate UNKNOWN:** only the FY1998 statement that the "reserve for sales returns … has been insignificant", and FY1999's disclosure of returns as a **netting instruction** ("net of returns and gift certificate discounts") with no amount — Date: FY1998 / FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998; 10-K FY1999 L1845-1846 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: "reserve for sales returns … has been insignificant" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. Cross-referenced with E88 (the policy is filed; the magnitude is not). [src: s3p2 §K.6 row 11]

K72 Claim: **The category revenue split is UNKNOWN as an audited line because the disaggregation is drawn and then withdrawn:** FY1999 defines reportable segments "based on product line information, considering line maturity" and then **aggregates US Books, Music and DVD/video into one line** "due to the similarity of their economic characteristics" (L4155-4162) — so the only *category* figures in the corpus are company press-release assertions: online music "SALES OF $14.4 MILLION" (Q3-1998) and "Music sales grew to $33.1 million, a 130 percent increase over sales of $14.4 million in the third quarter of 1998" plus "Video sales were strong following the store's opening on November 17" (Q4-1998), unaudited and unreconciled to any 10-Q — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L4155-4162, L4189-4191; `sources/8-K_event-1998-10-28_…`; `sources/8-K_event-1999-01-26_…` EX-99 L230-236 — Source date: 2000-03-23; 1998-10-28; 1999-01-26 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (segments as filed) + DOCUMENTED ABSENCE (category split) — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: the M-7 stale-null family (E94). **The video store's launch day 1998-11-17 is the only category-launch day in the corpus.** [src: s3p2 §K.6 row 12]

K73 Claim: **RETRACTION — FY1999 revenue per employee is UNKNOWN and must not be printed.** DERIVED on the face of it $1,639,839k ÷ 7,600 = $215.8k per head, and the temptation to print it is exactly the defect: the headcount bases are not comparable — **614 full-time (1997-12-31); "approximately 2,100 employees" (1998-12-31), no basis qualifier; "approximately 7,600 full-time and part-time employees" (1999-12-31)**, three dates and three measurement bases with the widest last, plus independent contractors and temporary personnel outside the count — Date: FY1999 (and FY1998) — Source: 10-K FY1999 L684-686 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: RETRACTION + DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: "approximately 7,600 full-time and part-time employees" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.161**. A 3.6× step from 2,100 to 7,600 is measured on a **widened** denominator, so revenue per employee would compare a narrower headcount against a broader one in the same ratio. The only defensible statement is that it is **not computable on a filed like-for-like basis**, and the same retraction applies to FY1998 ($609,819 ÷ "approximately 2,100", a figure the filing itself declines to define). [src: s3p2 §K.6 row 13; §K.6 retraction (i)]

K74 Claim: **Orders per customer, revenue per account and "LTV" remain UNKNOWN:** cumulative accounts (1.5m / 6.2m / 16.9m) are a **stock**, self-defined and — from Auctions onward — inclusive of marketplace registrants, so the base changes definition inside the stage; Stage 2's prohibition stands that cumulative revenue ÷ cumulative accounts mixes a flow with a stock and **may not be used as an LTV proxy** (S2B-66); customer acquisition cost is UNKNOWN because there is no new-customer count, no order count, no attribution of advertising to acquisition rather than branding, and advertising expense is printed **only annually** ($3.4m/$21.2m/$60.2m/$140.9m) in no 10-Q — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999; 10-K FY1998; (S2B-66 carried) — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. [src: s3p2 §K.6 rows 14–15]

K75 Claim: **Associates Program economics UNKNOWN, with counts only:** "Approximately 200,000 Web sites have enrolled" (FY1998) → "As of February 29, 2000, approximately 430,000 Web sites have enrolled" (FY1999) — a **post-window measurement date**, the annual report giving no Associates number at its own 1999-12-31 date — and no commission rate, commission expense, attributable revenue or order share exists in any filing in the window — Date: 1998-12-31; 2000-02-29 `(PB)` — Source: 10-K FY1998 L383-389; 10-K FY1999 L542-545 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.143** family (unit discontinuity). FY1999's expense captions contain no line into which an Associates fee could be seen; **Stage 2's verdict — efficacy claimed, never measured — is not cured by six more years of filings.** [src: s3p2 §K.6 row 16]

K76 Claim: **The cost of operating the DC estate is not separable from marketing by construction:** FY1998 repeats FY1997's definition putting distribution-centre and customer-service staffing into "marketing and sales", and FY1998's commitments table presents one combined column "OPERATING LEASES AND MARKETING AGREEMENTS" totalling $134,829k; the only separable item is total rental expense, $8.5m (1998) / $2.1m (1997) / $270,000 (1996) — Date: FY1996–FY1998 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L1390-1392, L2853 — Source date: 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (definition) + DOCUMENTED ABSENCE (separation) — Passage: "All fulfillment costs not included in cost of sales, including the cost of operating and staffing distribution centers and customer service, are included in marketing and sales" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-16 family. Cross-referenced with G112, F60. [src: s3p2 §K.6 row 17]

K77 Claim: **Retraction (ii), stated so it is not re-attempted downstream: the "$1 billion annualized sales level" is not an unstated-basis boast** — its carrier states the basis: "Net sales for the fourth quarter were **$252.9 million**, an increase of 283 percent over net sales of $66.0 million for the fourth quarter of 1997. **Based on fourth quarter sales**, Amazon.com has achieved a $1 billion annualized sales level three and one-half years after opening for business" — it is a **peak-quarter annualisation**, and the company's own seasonality disclosure declares Q4 the peak, so it is a *maximal* basis whose sign happened to be conservative — Date: 1999-01-26 — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1999-01-26_acc-0000891020-99-000103_filed-1999-01-27.txt` EX-99 L192-195; 10-K FY1999 L598-601 — Source date: 1999-01-26 — Tier: 1 — Class: ESTIMATE/DERIVED with a stated basis + RETRACTION of the mislabel — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 252.9 × 4 = 1,011.6. **Q1-1999 $293,643k and Q2-1999 $314,377k are both above the annualised $252.9m.** Still only ever asserted by the party with an interest. Likewise "combined sales in the U.K. and Germany nearly quadrupled **over the third quarter**" is a Q3→Q4-1998 sequential change in two stores that opened in October 1998, combined, with no absolute value — **not** an international-sales statement (ST3E-18). [src: s3p2 §K.6 retraction (ii)]

K78 Claim: **Stage 2's fourteen UNKNOWN money claims, re-searched on this pass over 99 files including the FY1999 10-K and its amendment: two closed outright (#7 shipping revenue, #9 fulfilment labour), five answered in part (#6, #8, #10, #11, #14), seven held (#1–#5, #12, #13) — and #3 is materially narrowed rather than unmoved** — Date: 1997-05-16 → 1999-12-31 — Source: §K.7 tally table against Stage 2 §K.9 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (the tally) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] the fourteen rows with their statuses — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Closure #9 is the single largest of Stage 3: the cost to serve became a filed number**, $12.1m → $50.3m → $188.4m printed for the first time in the FY1999 10-K MD&A (L1969-1972) inside the marketing-and-sales caption whose percentage of net sales the company prints as 27.1% → 21.8% → 25.2% (L1960). Held rows #1–#5, #12, #13 are registered individually at K66–K72. **New claims this stage adds to the register at UNKNOWN:** the $85.4m of employment-contingent acquisition consideration actually recorded as compensation expense; the identity of the officer who owes the $1,099k stock note; the FY1998 net-sales movement of $177k; the amount of the personal guarantees and the date of their release. [src: s3p2 §K.7]

K79 Claim: **§K.8's split-vintage table is the one arithmetic trap that manufactures false conflicts:** pre-1998-06-01 none (1×, the S-1 lineage and 424B1, FY1997 10-K per-share data with LPS $(1.27) *pro forma* on 21,651k shares); 1998-06-01 → 1999-01-03, 2-for-1 (record 1998-05-20, stock dividend) = 2× (Q1/Q2/Q3-1998 10-Qs, FY1997 10-K restated comparatives); 1999-01-04 → 1999-08-31, + 3-for-1 (announced 1998-11-19, record 1998-12-18) = 6× (FY1998 10-K, Q1/Q2-1999 10-Qs, the converts' $156.055 / 8,009,996, DEF 14A 1999-04-07); **1999-09-01 onward, + 2-for-1 paid 1999-09-01 = 12×** (Q3-1999 10-Q, FY1999 10-K and 10-K/A, 8-K event 1999-10-28) — Date: 1997-05-15 → 1999-12-31 — Source: as listed per vintage in `sources/` — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (vintages) + DERIVED (factors) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (each vintage is a set of documents about one corporate action; the cumulative factor is arithmetic) — Conflicts: **U.157**, and the §K.8 conflict below. [src: s3p2 §K.8 table]

K80 Claim: **The cumulative factor is 12×, not 6×, and the third split is documented in the filings but undocumented in this project's own registers,** which carry 6× as the cumulative factor: the split is evidenced only by (i) the Q2-1999 10-Q's forward footnote that a split "will have effect on September 1, 1999; accordingly, the stock split has not been reflected" (L655), (ii) the Q3-1999 10-Q's parenthetical "and the two-for-one stock split **paid** on September 1, 1999" (L124), (iii) FY1999 10-K L1692 and L4987, and (iv) the 8-K of 1999-10-28 reciting both the January 4 and September 1 splits (L521, L609) — Date: 1999-09-01 — Source: as listed — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "and the two-for-one stock split paid on September 1, 1999" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (**all four carriers are one corporate lineage, therefore one source**, but a primary one) — Conflicts: registered: **any inherited per-share row is wrong by a factor of exactly two and must be recomputed, not re-researched.** **Mechanical cross-check (the 6× reading closes to the dollar):** the FY1998 10-K's 1997 share count of 144,909k ÷ 2 ÷ 3 = 24,151.5k against the FY1997 report's as-filed 23,937,169 leaves a residual of **214,331 shares**, which is exactly the PlanetAll pooling addition — so the 12× extension is a confirmed reading rather than an assumption (S4 cross-check). [src: s3p2 §K.8 correction]

K81 Claim: **Second trap, same cause — FY1997's loss per share is printed $(1.27) in the FY1997 report, $(0.24) in the FY1998 report and $(0.12) in the FY1999 report**, on denominators of 21,651k (*pro forma*, pre-IPO-style), 130,341k and 260,682k: **the difference is the denominator's construction, not the loss**, and FY1997's EPS and FY1998's are different instruments whose year-over-year "change" is not interpretable — Date: FY1997 / FY1998 / FY1999 — Source: FY1997 10-K; FY1998 10-K; FY1999 10-K L2608-2646 — Source date: 1998-03-30; 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (three printings, three bases) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.159** family. Wherever a 1997 or 1998 per-share figure appears in Stage 3, **its vintage travels with it or the figure is not usable** (see also the 1999 option table: Dalzell's 1997 grant reads 125,000 in the 1998 proxy and 750,000 in the 1999 proxy — one grant, two vintages, **not** a second grant). [src: s3p2 §K.8 second trap; §K.1 row 8]

L. VALIDATION SIGNALS (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

**Geometry and honesty rule carried into every row of §L:** seven columns as in Stage 2 §L, and **every row must
fill the "did NOT" column with something true** — a row whose "did NOT" cell could be deleted without loss is a
row that has not been audited. Third-party commissions are admitted only with the L-8 caveat (they sit inside
net sales, so no take rate exists or can be built). **No §L record asserts that a signal made the business
profitable per customer; §K.6 holds that UNKNOWN at every date.** Standing reading limit as for §K.

L21 Claim: **The FY1998 audited year closes and the 1997 margin cut reverses:** net sales $609,996k (+312.8% DERIVED), gross profit $133,841k, margin **21.9%** against 19.5% (1997) and 22.0% (1996); loss from operations (111,960); net loss (124,546) — Date: 1999-03-05 — Source: `sources/10-K_FY1998_acc-0000891020-99-000375_filed-1999-03-05.txt` L1222-1247, L2016-2039 — Source date: 1999-03-05 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited) + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: L-1 vs ST3B §U2(a) (see L34). **What it demonstrated:** a fourth consecutive year of triple-digit revenue growth arriving **with** a gross-margin recovery, so the FY1997 pricing programme did not ratchet margin down permanently, and scale was auditable. **What it did NOT demonstrate:** that the recovery was trading improvement — part of the 1997→1998 comparability is the PlanetAll pooling recast itself (1997 gross profit moves +$5k but marketing and sales +$1,522k, so the restated base is not the 1997 a reader saw), part is mix, and the loss **widened by $94.5m in dollars** in the same year; and it says nothing about per-order profitability, there being no order count. [src: s3p2 §L row L-1; ST3B §S1]

L22 Claim: **The debt market opened at scale, unsecured:** 10% Senior Discount Notes due 2008, announced $275m and upsized 1998-05-05 to ~$326m gross / ~$315.7m net, principal at maturity $530m, **no cash interest before 2003-11-01**, change-of-control put at 101% of accreted value — Date: 1998-04-24 → 1998-05-05 — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1998-04-24_acc-0000891020-98-000653_filed-1998-04-27.txt`; `sources/8-K_event-1998-05-05_acc-0000891020-98-000722_filed-1998-05-06.txt`; `sources/424B2_final-prospectus_acc-0000891020-98-001279_filed-1998-08-13.txt` L1345-1360; `sources/10-Q_Q1-1998_…` EX-4.1 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] a 1996-vintage loss-making online retailer raised $326m of unsecured money with no cash coupon and no lien at a moment when the only secured money available had covenanted on its accounts-payable aging — Conf: High (terms); Medium (what the upsize priced) — Corroboration: 1 (8-Ks, the 424B2 and the 10-Q note are one financing sequence in one issuer's paper; **the 424B2 is not corroboration of the later 10-K summaries — same lineage**) — Conflicts: none. **Did NOT demonstrate:** that the market validated the business — it priced a deferral of cash interest, not an expectation of trading cash flow (the pre-working-capital subtotal is negative in all four years, K44); the upsizing happened in a 1998 bull bid for convertibles. [src: s3p2 §L row L-2; ST3B §F1]

L23 Claim: **$1.25bn converts completed 1999-02-03, announced $500m and increased the same day** — the equity-linked market took 2.5× the announced deal on the announcement day at a conversion price of $156.055 (6× vintage) = **$78.0275 on the final 12× vintage**, below every filed 1999 quarterly high — the largest single financing in the company's history to that date and the clearest external price signal in Stage 3 — Date: 1999-02-03 — Source: 8-K 1999-01-28 ×2; 8-K 1999-02-03 + EX-4.1/4.2; `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_…` Note 2 L1536-1560; 10-K FY1998 L1761-1766 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + ESTIMATE/DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High (amounts, terms); Medium (what the upsizing priced) — Corroboration: 1 (the two 1999-01-28 accessions are one event's paper) — Conflicts: **N-5's strike vs M.13 (L36)**. **Did NOT demonstrate repeatability of the trading model:** the notes are a bet on the *stock*, whose filed quarterly high fell from $110.63 (Q2-1999) to $85.00/$41.00 (Q3-1999) (FY1999 10-K L1672-1690); **the 150%-of-strike redemption gate ($117.04 DERIVED) is above every filed 1999 high** (M.13 → M40); and none of the $1.25bn demonstrably funded operations — $126.0m face of its own discount notes (accreted value $83.9m) was repurchased in Q1-1999 and FY1999 shows $178.4m of cash spent repurchasing them while operating cash flow was **−$90,875k**. [src: s3p2 §L row L-3]

L24 Claim: **The seasonal peak recurs a third time on the company's own release:** Q4-1998 net sales $252.9m "an increase of 283 percent over net sales of $66.0 million for the fourth quarter of 1997"; "Based on fourth quarter sales, Amazon.com has achieved a $1 billion annualized sales level three and one-half years after opening for business"; 6.2 million cumulative accounts; music $33.1m; video "strong following the store's opening on November 17"; UK+Germany "nearly quadrupled over the third quarter" — Date: 1999-01-26 (release), filed 1999-01-27 — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1999-01-26_acc-0000891020-99-000103_filed-1999-01-27.txt` EX-99 L192-195, L226-236 — Source date: 1999-01-27 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (a dated release) + SELF-MEASUREMENT — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High (that it was said, and when); **Low as measurement** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.162** (the day against the tables), the K77 retraction. **Did NOT demonstrate:** that the run rate forecasts a year — it annualises the company's self-declared peak quarter (FY1999 10-K L598-601), and every figure in the row is a company self-report in a company press release: the music and video dollars are unaudited and reconciled to no 10-Q, "nearly quadrupled" is a one-quarter sequential change in two stores opened in October 1998 with no absolute value, and the accounts figure is cumulative and self-defined. **Arithmetic:** 252,893 ÷ 609,996 = **41.4%** of FY1998 in Q4; 252.9 × 4 = 1,011.6. [src: s3p2 §L row L-4; ST3E-18, ST3E-19]

L25 Claim: **Two foreign stores open and the segment note sizes them:** UK (Slough ~41,000 sq ft) and Germany (Regensburg ~32,000 sq ft) leases filed at 1998-12-31, sites launched October 1998; International segment revenue $21,806k = **3.6% of FY1998 net sales** (DERIVED), International segment loss $(25,498)k, foreign long-lived assets $2.8m; "Sales to customers outside of the US represented approximately 20%" — Date: 1998-10 (launch) / 1998-12-31 (audited) — Source: 10-K FY1998 Item 2; 10-K FY1999 Note 14 L4189, L4200, L2605-2646 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High (leases, segment dollars); High **that the 20% is not an organisation** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.160**/the international-caption family (F70/F71). **Did NOT demonstrate materiality, or the interpretation of the 20%:** the overseas business was 3.6% of sales and $2.8m of assets, so the 20% is overwhelmingly **US export sales — a shipping fact, not an organisation**; nor that foreign trading worked, the segment losing money in both filed years ($(25,498)k 1998, $(79,223)k 1999); and the later "fell 33% → 25% → 20%" claim is a **share-versus-flow error** (→ M31). [src: s3p2 §L row L-5; ST3E-05, ST3E-22]

L26 Claim: **A third-party marketplace takes its first money** — Auctions launched March 1999, zShops October 1999 (the Q3-1999 10-Q says "late September 1999"), sothebys.amazon.com and the UK/German mirrors November 1999; from Q2-1999 net sales "include auctions revenue, which is comprised of placement fees and sales commissions on closed auctions"; Q4-1999 "surpassed a combined 1 million registered users and 1.5 million listings" — Date: 1999-03 → 2000-03-23 (disclosure) — Source: `sources/10-Q_Q2-1999_…` L852-854; `sources/10-Q_Q3-1999_…` L863-865; 10-K FY1999 L1848-1850, L265-286, L340-350 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + NEGATIVE RESULT — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High (recognition, dates); **UNKNOWN (volume, take rate)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.155, U.160**. **Did NOT demonstrate that a marketplace transaction economy existed in any measurable size, and it cannot be measured:** (i) commissions and placement fees sit inside net sales, so a take rate is structurally unknowable; (ii) **placement fees are earned whether or not anything sells** — listing revenue, the least transaction-validated money a trading platform can take; (iii) the count is "registered users", not sellers, undisclosed by service and self-reported; (iv) it is dated to a quarter **after** both narrower candidate endpoints. [src: s3p2 §L row L-6; ST3E-02, ST3E-13, ST3E-34]

L27 Claim: **Fulfilment cost becomes a filed number for the first time:** $12.1m (1997) → $50.3m (1998) → $188.4m (1999); marketing and sales 27.1% → 21.8% → 25.2% of net sales (company-printed, L1960); fulfilment ÷ net sales DERIVED 8.19% → 8.25% → 11.49% — Date: 1998-06-11 / 1998-11-17 launches → FY1999 MD&A — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1960, L1964-1972, L1980-1990, L1993-2000 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: as quoted at G77 — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-16, **U.158** family. **Did NOT demonstrate anything favourable:** it is **the only component of Stage 2's fourteen that closed, and it closed adverse** — fulfilment grew 15.6× in two years against net sales 11.1×, the 1998 percentage "improvement" is the same decomposition effect M.3 refuses to call efficiency, it is still not per order, and the FY1999 note warns standard-setters were reviewing "the financial statement classification of … fulfillment and order processing costs" by e-commerce companies "**including Amazon.com**" (L1993-2000): the line was about to be re-cut. [src: s3p2 §L row L-7; ST3E-20]

L28 Claim: **Repeat purchase, measured at the same quarter two years running:** "The percentage of orders by repeat customers increased from 64% in the fourth quarter of 1998 to 73% in the fourth quarter of 1999"; cumulative accounts 1.5m (1997) → 6.2m (1998) → 16.9m (1999); "over 17 million customer accounts in over 150 countries" — Date: FY1999 MD&A, printed 2000-03-23 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1861-1863, L198 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + SELF-MEASUREMENT — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High as a filed statement; **Low as a retention curve** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.161**, M-9/M-10 families. **Did NOT demonstrate that retention is measured:** "repeat customer" is undefined in every filing, the base is a percentage of an undisclosed order total, and the account denominator changed definition inside the stage to include marketplace registrants ("inclusive of Auctions" onward), so 16.9m is not the same object as 6.2m; and it cannot be annualised, because Q4 is the peak quarter, so two Q4s are two **best** quarters. [src: s3p2 §L row L-8; ST3E-09, ST3E-28]

L29 Claim: **Three stock splits in fifteen months, the third evidenced only in quarterly footnotes:** 2-for-1 (record 1998-05-20, effected 1998-06-01); 3-for-1 (announced 1998-11-19, record 1998-12-18, effected 1999-01-04); 2-for-1 paid 1999-09-01 — cumulative **12×**, not 6× — Date: 1998-06-01 / 1999-01-04 / 1999-09-01 — Source: `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_…` L453-458; `sources/10-Q_Q2-1999_…` L655; `sources/10-Q_Q3-1999_…` L124; 10-K FY1999 L1692, L4987; 8-K event 1999-10-28 L521 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "and the two-for-one stock split paid on September 1, 1999" — Conf: High (existence, dates); **Low as a validation signal** — Corroboration: 1 (one corporate lineage) — Conflicts: **U.157**; and the registers carry 6× (K80). **Did NOT demonstrate anything about the underlying business:** a split redistributes the same equity, changes no cash flow and is not audited evidence of value creation; its one forensic use is defensive — it is why every per-share figure in this stage is vintage-bound, and the third split is **unannounced in any 8-K in the corpus**. [src: s3p2 §L row L-9]

L30 Claim: **Founder alignment, filed twice from two directions:** Bezos's salary $81,840 for 1998 with $0 bonus and no option grants against Dalzell $201,512, Aposporos $142,083, Spiegel $116,352, Risher $105,168 — the CEO paid 41% of the highest-paid VP in the same table; and the converts' conversion price $156.055, above the market price at which they were sold — Date: 1999-04-07 (proxy) / 1999-02-03 (converts) — Source: `sources/DEF14A_1999-proxy-statement_acc-0000891020-99-000635_filed-1999-04-07.txt`; `sources/DEF14A_1998-proxy-statement_acc-0000891020-98-000601_filed-1998-04-17.txt`; 10-K FY1998 L1989 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + NEGATIVE RESULT — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High (amounts); **UNKNOWN (motive, the officer note)** — Corroboration: 2 (a proxy table and an executed indenture term are documents of different purpose; the pay row and the strike row are independent of each other) — Conflicts: none. **Did NOT demonstrate that alignment was *chosen* for that reason** — no document states a motive (§N's evidentiary limit), and the table's silence is a disclosure convention, not a policy statement; nor that he took no other value (the $1,099k officer note, borrower unnamed); nor that the pay comparison is like-for-like across officers on different hire dates. This is the sharpest incentive-alignment evidence in Stage 3 and it is Tier-1 and contemporaneous in both documents. [src: s3p2 §L row L-10; ST3B §K1-§K2]

L31 Claim: **Physical commitment, irreversible by the count date:** Fernley ~323,000 sq ft "highly mechanized distribution facility" leased December 1998 expiring 2009; five Buschman Sales Agreements of 1999-03-11 (prices redacted under Rule 24b-2) covering Fernley Phases I-II plus three undetermined "Site A/B/C" proposals; FY1999 capex $287,055k, gross fixed assets $43,585k → $366,977k (8.4×), CIP $1,760k → $83,290k (22.7%), leased assets $442k → $52,374k (118.5×), ~3.8 million sq ft across eight US DCs against 285,000 sq ft at 1997-12-31 — Date: 1998-12 (lease) / 1999-03-11 (contracts) / FY1999 (capex) — Source: 10-K FY1998 Item 2, Note 6; `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_…` EX-10.1 L1810-1813; 10-K FY1999 L2852, L3487-3501, L573-574, L1604-1605 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED + REDACTED — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] a 15.4× linear expansion of filed floor area financed with equipment contracts and an eleven-year lease — Conf: High (leases, cash-flow lines); Medium (sq-ft sums); **UNKNOWN (equipment price, throughput)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-13, M-15. **Did NOT demonstrate that the network was in operation:** over half the filed 1998 US floor area was leased but not yet operating at the balance-sheet date and $83.3m of the 1999 estate was still construction in progress at 1999-12-31, immediately before the first automated peak season that produced the $39m of Q4-1999 inventory charges; square footage is not throughput, and no capacity, cycle-time or order-throughput figure exists anywhere in the window. [src: s3p2 §L row L-11; ST3D-30, ST3E-33]

L32 Claim: `(PB)`, printed 2000-03-23 — **the one audited disaggregation of the new businesses, and it is not flattering:** FY1999 "Early-Stage Businesses and Other" revenue $163.8m, **gross loss $7.8m**, segment loss **$242.1m**; equity in losses of equity-method investees $(76,769)k; amortisation of goodwill and other intangibles $214,694k (nil in 1997); merger, acquisition and investment-related costs $8,072k cash portion after $47,065k non-cash in 1998 — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 Note 14 L4155-4162, L4189-4191; L2605-2646 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited) + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High (figures); **Medium-Low as evidence about the businesses** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Did NOT demonstrate that the expansion strategy works:** it is the single most adverse audited fact in the stage — the segment on which Stage 3's expansion narrative rests did not cover even its own **cost of sales** in its first full year — and equally it does not demonstrate the ventures failed, no unit data existing; it is a first-year, differently-defined, company-constructed segment whose composition the note does not fully specify. [src: s3p2 §L row L-12; ST3E-12]

L33 Claim: §L's knowability verdict: what is knowable is every filed date, price, coupon, covenant, split, lease and dollar line, and every sentence the company printed about demand; **not knowable** is whether any of these measured behaviour at unit level — Date: 1997-05-16 → 1999-12-31 — Source: §L rows as registered — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] Stage-3 "validation" is necessarily about prices paid for securities, dollars committed to infrastructure, and claims filed; what is genuinely new in Stage 3 is that **a dollar cost of serving customers became filed**, which converts one question from unknowable to answerable — and the answer is adverse — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: none. *UNKNOWN, held open:* the marketplace's volume and take rate; the Associates yield; the $1.25bn's marginal use; whether the Fernley automation performed before the Q4-1999 charges; every retention figure's denominator. [src: s3p2 §L knowability]

L34 Claim: **Registered conflict, §L row L-1 vs ST3B §U2(a):** the finance dossier's quarterly gross-margin ladder (Q4-1997 19.6% → Q4-1998 21.1%) supports the margin-recovery row, but its Q4-1998 cell is drawn from Note 12 of the FY1998 10-K while the annual figure the row prints ($609,996) is restated to $609,819 in the FY1999 10-K, so the quarterly and annual bases are two vintages of one year and **the recovery may be overstated by up to ~0.1pt**; not resolvable on the local record — Date: 1998 — Source: 10-K FY1998 Note 12 vs 10-K FY1999 L1732-1761 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (basis conflict) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High that the bases differ — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: emitted to §U (the vintage family; cf. **U.159**). [src: s3p2 §L conflict 1]

L35 Claim: **Registered conflict, §L row L-9's third split:** no 8-K announcing the 1999-09-01 2-for-1 exists in the corpus, while four later filings and the FY1999 10-K/A option table treat it as fact — to be registered as a **documented-by-inference event, not an announced one** — Date: 1999-09-01 — Source: K79/K80 evidence set — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DOCUMENTED ABSENCE (the announcing instrument) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.157**; `stage_3_part_2.md` §L `CONFLICT FOR §U`. [src: s3p2 §L conflict 2]

M. NEGATIVE SIGNALS AND FAILURES (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

*This section is the load-bearing one: Stage 3 is otherwise a growth story told by the survivor's own filings, which is exactly the reading §2 forbids. Nothing here is scored, and nothing is offered as a reason the stage failed — the stage did not fail; the record shows a company whose revenue grew 169% in its last stage year while its operating loss grew 455%.*

M27 Claim: **Fulfilment economics moved the wrong way and the filings now prove it in dollars:** fulfilment costs $12.1m → $50.3m → $188.4m, i.e. 15.6× in two years against net sales 11.1×, and 8.19% → 8.25% → **11.49%** of sales — the cost of delivering an order rose 3.24 points of revenue in the single year the network doubled, after holding flat through the year of the biggest facility programme to date — Date: FY1997 → FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1969-1972, L1980-1984, L1988-1992 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "Marketing and sales expenses increased as a percentage of net sales in 1999 … primarily due to increases in fulfillment expenses associated with our expansion of our distribution center network and customer service staff during 1999" — Conf: High (movement); **UNKNOWN (decomposition)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-16. *Mechanism:* the company's own MD&A; the forward sentence "we … expect that fulfillment costs will decline as a percentage of sales in the future as additional capacity of our existing distribution center network is more fully utilized" **declares an efficiency that had not occurred**, and its premise is an admission of idle capacity in the year the fixed-asset base went 8.4×. *Alternative on the record:* **mix, and it is filed, not inferred** — "the operational challenges of shipping non-uniform and heavy products as part of the fulfillment of toy, electronics, home improvement and other product orders" (L1033-1036), those stores launched July and November 1999. [src: s3p2 §M.1]

M28 Claim: **The year trading generated no cash, and the "self-funding" reading died inside the stage:** the printed pre-working-capital subtotal is $(26,160)k (1997), $(41,433)k (1998), $(320,987)k (1999), so FY1998's +$31,035k sits inside a $(41,433)k hole filled by +$72,468k of working-capital change, payables alone being 2.53× the reported inflow; FY1999 then falsifies the reading — payables supplied +$330,166k and total float +$230,112k, the largest in the company's history, and operating cash flow was still $(90,875)k — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L2257-2258, L2831-2832; 10-K FY1998 L2229-2270 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited) + DERIVED — Passage: "Net cash used by operating activities amounted to $90.9 million for 1999" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **the removal of "first positive operating cash flow" from the validation register** (ST3E-21 / OC-205; K46). *Mechanism:* payables scale with purchases and purchases with inventory, which consumed $(172,069)k in 1999 — **the float chased the stock instead of funding it**; DPO 101.3 → 86.8 → 125.2 days, i.e. terms tightened in the year the claim was available and stretched 38 days in the year it stopped being. *Alternative:* ordinary retail working-capital behaviour at 4× growth — unanswered, because no vendor contract guaranteeing terms appears in any filing. [src: s3p2 §M.2]

M29 Claim: **Advertising's fall to 8.60% of sales is a decomposition effect, not efficiency, while the line containing it rose:** advertising $3.4m (1996) / $21.2m (1997) / $60.2m (1998) / $140.9m (1999) — 14.3% → 9.87% → 8.60% of net sales — but advertising and fulfilment share one caption and that caption's percentage of net sales **rose from 21.8% (1998) to 25.2% (1999)** (company-printed) in the same year advertising's share fell 1.3 points, because the other component grew 3.75× while sales grew 2.7× — Date: FY1996–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1960, L2884-2885, L1993-2000 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED + RULING — Passage: "Equity securities of other companies received for non-cash revenue for advertising and promotional services … $54,402" — Conf: High (all inputs printed) — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: registered (ST3E-20 / OC-206). **Ruling: no advertising-efficiency claim may be made for any year in this stage**; two contaminants: about a third of 1999's advertising was settled at least partly in **barter** ($54,402k of equity securities received), and accrued advertising moved +$42,382k in 1999 against +$9,617k in 1998. **The Stage-2-derived "net sales per dollar of advertising" ladder (4.6× / 7.0× / 10.1×) is RETRACTED as an efficiency claim** for FY1998 onward, retained if at all only as a spend-to-revenue ratio with this row attached. [src: s3p2 §M.3]

M30 Claim: **Q4-1999: the company wrote off inventory and named the failure — approximately $39 million**, in the MD&A and repeated in the risk factor three lines below the company's own advance warning of that exact failure mode — Date: 1999 Q4 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1890-1892, L1049-1050, L1042-1048, L1033-1036 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (incurred, not conditional) + DERIVED — Passage: "We may fail to accurately predict the optimal inventory levels at our distribution centers for the fourth quarter … If we overstock products, we may be required to take significant inventory mark-downs or write-offs, which could reduce gross margins"; then "In the fourth quarter of 1999, we incurred inventory-related charges, which significantly decreased our gross margins" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (MD&A and risk factor are one document; **the risk factor itself states no causal link and no amount**) — Conflicts: M-13. **Arithmetic:** $39m = 2.4% of FY1999 net sales and **13.4% of the year's entire gross profit** (39,000 ÷ 290,645); the larger part of why margin fell 21.9% → 17.7% while the company still described itself as growing 169%. *Mechanism, filed:* a first stocking decision in two brand-new categories in the peak quarter across a network 22.7% of whose gross fixed assets were still CIP. *Alternative:* a one-off on the **first** attempt at what the network was built for (FY1998's comparative shows no charge), available and not falsified. [src: s3p2 §M.4]

M31 Claim: **The international "fall" 33% → 25% → 20% is a share-versus-flow error** — international dollars grew 7.1× then 3.3× (segment revenue $21,806k 1998 → $167,743k 1999) while the share of a faster-growing total fell — Date: FY1996–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L1325-1326; 10-K FY1999 Note 14 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: DERIVED (correction of a reading) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: the M-12/F70 family. [src: s3p2 §M.7/§L row 5 note]

M32 Claim: **The automation inexperience was admitted in writing and the admission was then softened in the same corpus, before it could be read against the charge** — a sequence of three filings, each on a primary document — Date: 1999-11-15 → 2000-03-23 → 1999-03-11 (contracts) — Source: `sources/10-Q_Q3-1999_…` L1626-1635; `sources/10-K_FY1999_…` L1020-1032; `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_…` EX-10.1-10.5, L1678-1681, L1810-1813 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (three dated states) — Passage: Q3-1999: "These distribution centers are or will be highly automated and **we have no previous experience with automated distribution centers**, as the two distribution centers in operation prior to 1999, in Washington and Delaware, were manually operated. The new distribution centers may fail to operate properly to ensure that customer demand is met, **particularly during the fourth quarter of 1999**."; FY1999: "we have had **limited experience** with automated distribution centers. The two distribution centers we operated prior to 1999 … **are** manually operated … We are not experienced in coordinating and managing distribution operations in geographically distant locations." … "we may over-expand our facilities, which may result in excess inventory", warehousing, fulfillment and distribution capacity." — Conf: High on all three strings and on the softening — Corroboration: 1 (same document family re-stating itself; **the differential is the evidence**) — Conflicts: registered; see G92/G93. **"No previous experience" became "limited experience" between November 1999 and March 2000 without any stated change of fact — a softened admission, not a withdrawn one, and the register must carry both wordings and both dates.** Note also the tense: the 10-Q says the old DCs **were** manual; the 10-K says they **are**, i.e. at 1999-12-31 two of the estate's buildings were still manual. The contracts predate the sites (three undetermined "Site A/B/C"), and the prices are redacted, so the capital cost of the automation is UNKNOWN at any request budget. ST3E-33 confirms the five exhibits are **equipment purchase contracts, not marketplace/merchant contracts**, which is what the intake use-note had called them. [src: s3p2 §M.5]

M33 Claim: **This section does not support that the automation failed:** no throughput, cycle-time, error-rate or capacity figure exists at any date in the window, so the performance of the automated estate is UNKNOWN; M.4's inventory charge is the nearest the record comes, and **its attribution to the DCs rather than to the buying decision is not filed** — Date: 1997-05-16 → 1999-12-31 — Source: absence — Source date: n/a — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: none. [src: s3p2 §M.5 close]

M34 Claim: **The acquisition ledger does not price what it appears to price — finding 1: up to $85.4m of 1999's stated "price" is salary.** FY1999: "the Company has conditioned a portion of the overall consideration on the continued tenure of key employees … a maximum of **$85.4 million** in additional consideration relating to the Company's acquisitions for 1999 may be recorded as **compensation expense** … Amounts will be 'earned' based on tenure of certain employees and will be recognized as expense over a period of **12-36 months**" — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L3307-3319 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (audited note) — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: registered (ST3E-16, ST3E-15). *Reading:* the headline value of a 1999 deal is, up to that amount, retention pay contingent on people staying — not a purchase price and not a capitalised asset. **How much was actually recorded as compensation is UNKNOWN on the local record.** [src: s3p2 §M.6 item 1]

M35 Claim: **Finding 2: the audited stock consideration is larger than the announced price by $129.4m** — supplemental cash-flow "Stock issued in connection with business acquisitions $774,409 (1999) / $217,241 (1998) / $— (1997)" against the Q1-1999 Note 7's "approximately $645 million … mostly of common stock" for the four contemplated 1999 deals (the same package appears as ~$250m for Alexa/e-Niche/Exchange in the April 8-K); the audited line also carries Tool Crib and Back to Basics (each ~$156m, in no Stage-3 dossier before ST3E-15) and $44.1m of "immaterial" deals — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L2875; `sources/10-Q_Q1-1999_…` Note 7 — Source date: 2000-03-23; 1999-05-17 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: registered. **The announced figure is not the year's acquisition cost and must not be re-used as one.** [src: s3p2 §M.6 item 2]

M36 Claim: **Finding 3: for 1998 no component of the announced number is an accounting price at all** — announced Junglee + PlanetAll "aggregate value of approximately $280 million" against audited $217,241k of stock issued in 1998, a recorded Junglee purchase price of "approximately $180 million" (substantially all allocated to goodwill and other purchased intangibles, amortised over ~3 years), and **PlanetAll pooled — carrying no purchase price whatsoever**: a market-value-of-stock announcement at an undisclosed reference price, split across two different accounting methods — Date: 1998 — Source: `sources/10-Q_Q2-1998_…`; 10-K FY1998; 10-K FY1999 L2875 — Source date: 1998-08-14; 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "aggregate value of approximately $280 million" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: E78, K60. *Mechanism:* a stock-funded acquirer in a rising market announces at the announcement price and accounts at the closing price, or not at all if the deal pools. *Alternative:* the announced figures were honest snapshots, comparable to each other but not to the audited lines — broadly true, and exactly why they cannot be summed with them. [src: s3p2 §M.6 item 3]

M37 Claim: **The goodwill machine, and what it did to the loss:** amortisation of goodwill and other purchased intangibles nil (1997) → $42,599k (1998) → **$214,694k (1999)**; goodwill and other purchased intangibles net — (1997) → $186,377k (1998); equity in losses of equity-method investees $(76,769)k (nil in 1997), of which the ~46% **drugstore.com** stake is the named FY1998 item — Date: FY1997–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998; 10-K FY1999 L2605-2646 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 214,694 ÷ 1,639,839 = **13.1% of FY1999 net sales**; 214,694 ÷ 605,755 = **35.4% of the year's operating loss**, on lives averaging ~3 years as filed for Junglee. *Reading:* the loss line Stage 3 must not treat as trading deterioration is largely the accounting cost of the acquisition programme; conversely the programme's economic cost is not confined to that line — the same purchases removed $774.4m of equity without a dollar of cash. *Alternative:* amortisation is non-cash and the capitalisation aggressive rather than informative; the record cannot choose, no impairment test, cash-flow test or segment value figure existing in any filing. [src: s3p2 §M.7]

M38 Claim: **The pro-forma block is the sharpest available measure of what the programme did, and is never used as if audited:** the FY1998 pro-forma, expressly "not prepared in accordance with generally accounting principles" (L1610-1612 as printed, sic), shows net loss $(194,269)k (1998) vs $(124,546)k reported and **$(1,031,925)k (1999) vs $(719,968)k reported** — as if all acquisitions had closed on 1 January 1999, the loss would have been $312m larger — Date: FY1998 / FY1999 — Source: FY1998 10-K L1610-1612; FY1999 10-K L3974-3975 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (non-GAAP, labelled) — Passage: "not prepared in accordance with generally accounting principles" — Conf: High (printed); class differs from audited — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. Excluded from the audited series throughout. **Arithmetic:** 1,031,925 − 719,968 = 311,957 ≈ $312m. [src: s3p2 §M.7]

M39 Claim: **The personal guarantees: documented silence, and the EDGAR route is exhausted.** Searches across all 99 files for `Seafirst`, `Wells Fargo`, `Bezos … guarantee`, `release … guarantee` hit **only in the 1997 registration lineage** (S-1 original and Amendments Nos. 1-6 and the 424B1), where the three guarantees (Nov-1994→Dec-1996; Jul-1995; Apr-1995) and the **EX-10.27 Subrogation Agreement dated 1996-06-19** are described; **zero** guarantee or release hits in any filing after 1997-05-15 — Date: 1997-05-16 → 2000-03-23 — Source: negative searches across `sources/`; S-1 lineage EX-10.27 — Source date: 1997-03-24 → 1997-05-15 — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: "The Company intends to secure releases of all of Mr. Bezos' guarantees as soon as possible following the closing of this offering" — Conf: High (absence) — Corroboration: n/a (double-searched) — Conflicts: **U.167**. The only post-IPO `Seafirst` occurrence is a lease clause using Seafirst's prime rate to compute a **late charge on rent** (Q1-1998 10-Q), not related-party disclosure; the FY1997 10-K405 never names Seafirst or a Bezos guarantee; FY1998 Item 13 routes to the 1999 proxy, which is silent; FY1999 is silent. **Why more requests cannot settle it: the guarantees themselves were never filed** — the S-1 exhibit set's only bank-facing instrument is the Subrogation Agreement, Bezos against Amazon, not the bank against Bezos. **Status: UNKNOWN, now UNKNOWN-with-the-searches-recorded and the route marked exhausted. Nothing in this dataset may be quoted as saying the guarantees survived; nothing says they ended. Do not convert the silence into a finding either way — the attack that tried (ST3E-F1) failed.** [src: s3p2 §M.8]

M40 Claim: **The supply side ran through the same dependency, and the acquirer of the supplier was a competitor:** B&N's pending purchase of Ingram disclosed as a supply fact; rivals "may be able to secure merchandise from vendors on more favorable terms"; the FY1998 commitments table makes the estate's forward cost non-separable ("OPERATING LEASES AND MARKETING AGREEMENTS", $134,829k); payables tripled to $463,026k in 1999 against a company with **no owned real estate in either year** and, from 1998, a first-priority lien previously granted over substantially all assets — Date: 1998–1999 — Source: 10-K FY1998 L2844-2853, Item 2; 10-K FY1999 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT — Passage: "The Company does not own any real estate as of December 31, 1998" — Conf: High on the filed facts; **the concentration percentage for 1998-99 is UNKNOWN** (Ingram is no longer quantified post-IPO, so Stage 2's 58% cannot be extended) — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. Cross-referenced with G105–G112, I37. [src: s3p2 §M.9]

M41 Claim: **The demand-side quantities are all issuer-counted, and the disclosure stopped where measurement would have begun.** Set aside every issuer count and what survives Stage 3 is the financial, legal and capital spine — the filings, the instruments, the leases, the segment notes — **and nothing about demand** — Date: 1997-05-16 → 1999-12-31 — Source: F43–F51, K66–K75; 10-K FY1999; Media Metrix and Opinion Research quotations in company documents — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: DOCUMENTED ABSENCE — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: ST3E-28. What Stage 3 lacks: **any order count, any visit or conversion figure after 1997, any GMV, any take rate, any category revenue line, any returns amount, and a single number from a counterparty.** That is not a gap in this research; it is a **1999 disclosure fact**, and it is why "validation" in Stage 3 means prices paid for paper and dollars committed. [src: s3p2 §M.10]

M42 Claim: **Comparability defects that must be carried, not averaged — five of them:** (i) headcount basis changed at each of the three filed dates (K73); (ii) every 1997/1996 comparative moves twice, and **depreciation is printed three times with three values** (FY1997 10-K 3,388; FY1998 10-K 3,442 for 1997 and 9,692 for 1998; FY1999 10-K **9,421** for 1998 → **U.D1**); (iii) **FY1997 technology spend is three different numbers** 12,485 → 13,916 → 13,384 (→ **U.D6**), so any derived ratio moves up to 11% on the choice of document; (iv) **option balances differ by exactly 2×** at the same date, 27,332k vs 54,664k outstanding at 1997-12-31, resolved by instrument — the FY1999 report applying the September 1999 split retroactively (→ **U.D2**); (v) **the 10-K/A restated cash equivalents for all periods and FY1998's "change in cash" flips from +23,685 to −38,536** (ST3E-35) — Date: FY1996–FY1999 — Source: as listed — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (five pairs) — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 (all pairs are printings of one registrant's statements: **each difference is a version conflict, never corroboration**) — Conflicts: **U.D1→U.145**, **U.D2→U.146**, **U.D6→U.150**, **U.154**, U.158, U.159 family. **Each of these is a reason no cross-year ratio in Stage 3 may be printed without its document and its date, and each is emitted to §U rather than resolved here.** [src: s3p2 §M.11]

M43 Claim: **Litigation and the trade's own reaction, evidenced as noticed rather than as harm:** FY1998 Item 3 carries (a) Wal-Mart's trade-secret and employee-solicitation claims, refiled in Seattle January 1999, with Amazon counterclaiming — the only place in the corpus where a store-based retailer asserts Amazon's operating know-how as its own protectable interest; and (b) the independent-bookseller suit (*The Intimate Bookshop and Wallace Kuralt v. B&N, Borders, Amazon.com et al.*, ~$11.25m actual damages pleaded, trebled, seeking disgorgement of "allegedly discriminatory discounts, rebates and deductions"), **dismissed with prejudice on the plaintiffs' own motion 1999-03-04** after being amended to drop the class allegations — Date: 1997 → 1999-03-04 — Source: 10-K FY1998 Item 3 L1100-1130; `sources/10-Q_Q2-1998_…` Item 1 L1450-1472 — Source date: 1999-03-05; 1998-08-14 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (litigation) — Passage: as quoted — Conf: High on the filings; **UNKNOWN on all outcomes** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. *Negative signal, stated narrowly:* Amazon's discount architecture was **externally noticed by the trade itself**; the dismissal means nothing about merits, so no substantive outcome may be recorded (outcome: no adjudication). **The B&N false-advertising suit of 1997-05-12 remains UNRESOLVED in Stage 3:** the FY1997 10-K reports no material proceeding and the suit then vanishes; the FY1998 and FY1999 Item 3s do not name it either — **a retired disclosure is not a resolved condition.** [src: s3p2 §M.12]

M44 Claim: **The market's own verdict, in the filings, turned before the stage ended:** the FY1999 10-K's price-range table is stated on the **final 12× vintage** and prints 1998 $7.35/$4.15, $17.46/$6.43, $24.50/$10.83, $60.31/$13.33 and 1999 **$99.56/$42.13, $110.63/$44.88, $85.00/$41.00, $113.00/$61.00** — Date: FY1998–FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L1672-1690 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: "adjusted to reflect the 2-for-1 stock split effected June 1, 1998, the 3-for-1 stock split effected January 4, 1999, and the 2-for-1 stock split effected September 1, 1999" — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **N-5's strike vs M.13** (both true of the same instrument; the §U row must state which question each answers — investor willingness vs business validation). *Against §L's converts row:* the high fell 23.2% (110.63 → 85.00) and the low 8.7% from Q2 to Q3 1999, the Q4 high of $113.00 did not regain the Q2 high, the strike $78.0275 was in the money in every 1999 quarter, but **the optional redemption gate at 150% of strike = $117.04 (78.0275 × 1.5) is above every filed high in 1998 or 1999**, closest $113.00 in Q4-1999 — so the instrument behaved as 5-year-deferred debt at a 4¾% cash coupon with a redemption option the company could not yet exercise. *Against any claim of no confidence:* the notes closed 2.5× the ask in six days and the strike sat below all four 1999 highs. *Mechanism for the Q2→Q3 fall:* **UNKNOWN on the local record** — the filings offer no explanation of their own price ranges and no analyst, index or third-party document is in `sources/`; **UNTRIED:** contemporaneous market reporting of the September 1999 decline (named as an unmet retrieval need, not searched). *What this does not demonstrate:* that the financing was good or bad — the equity's post-stage fate is outside this record and is not imported. [src: s3p2 §M.13]

M45 Claim: **§M's framing datum, stated so that no record in it is read as a failure verdict:** the stage did not fail; on the filed numbers revenue grew 169% in its last year while the operating loss grew 455% — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L2605-2646 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: High — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. **Arithmetic:** 1,639,839 ÷ 609,819 = 2.689 → +168.9%; 605,755 ÷ 109,055 = 5.55 → +455%. [src: s3p2 §M preamble]

N. FOUNDER DECISIONS (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

*Twelve columns as in Stage 2 §N (`Date | Decision | State before | Information available | Unknowns |
Alternatives | Constraints | Rationale | Expected result | Actual result | Evidence | Confidence`) are carried
inside each record's claim and note fields; the appendix geometry fixes the field names.*
**Evidentiary limit, better than Stage 2's and stated plainly:** Stage 2's §N could fill *Rationale* only from
executed documents or `RETRO`; Stage 3 has two contemporaneous founder-state documents — the annual reports to
shareholders of 1997 (acc. 0000891020-98-000600, filed 1998-04-17) and 1998 (acc. 0000891020-99-000637, filed
1999-04-07), each closing "/s/ JEFFREY P. BEZOS / Jeffrey P. Bezos / Founder and Chief Executive Officer /
Amazon.com, Inc." — which are **FOUNDER CLAIM (contemporaneous)** in method §3's sense. Three limits remain and
are enforced in every row: (i) a shareholder letter explains choices, does not record deliberation and never
names a rejected option; (ii) the Sheff/*Playboy* interview is **conducted 1999, published 2000** and ranks
below the ARS for any claim about what Bezos believed in-window (ST3A-13's ordering, adopted); (iii) **no board
minute, plan document or option list survives in the corpus**, so `Unknowns` in every row still carries
"whether the alternatives were considered at all". §2's record-selection null applies in full: only the chosen
path was printed.

N20 Claim: **Decision — replace covenanted secured bank debt with unsecured discount notes** (1997-11-07 → 1998-05-05). State before: the $75.0m three-year senior secured facility drawn December 1997 (first-priority lien on substantially all assets, LIBOR + 3.50/4.00%, covenants on minimum cash, EBITDA, capex **and accounts-payable aging**, 50% sweep of any future raise, dividends prohibited, 750,000 warrants at $52.11), FY1997 net sales $147,758k, cash $109,810k — Date: 1997-11-07 → 1998-05-05 — Source: 8-K 1997-11-07; 8-K 1998-04-24; 8-K 1998-05-05; `sources/424B2_final-prospectus_acc-0000891020-98-001279_filed-1998-08-13.txt` L1355-1360; 10-K FY1998 L1519-1527, L2256-2264 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (acts, amounts) — Passage: "approximately $75.0 million of such proceeds to retire the Senior Loan and expects to use the remaining net proceeds for general corporate purposes, including working capital to fund anticipated [operating losses]" — Conf: High (acts, amounts); **Medium on the covenant motive**, inferred from the ordering of the company's own use-of-proceeds sentence rather than stated — Corroboration: 1 (one financing sequence; the 424B2 is part of the S-4 lineage's paper, not an independent origin) — Conflicts: none. Alternatives: a rights or public common issue; a smaller notes tranche; drawing the facility's **$100 million accordion**; more vendor and lease financing of the GE Capital type used 1997-02-12. Constraints: the 50% sweep made a second financing partly a repayment to the lender; the aging covenant constrained the one working-capital line that funded the year. Expected: cash-free debt service until 2003 and the removal of a lender's right to police payables. Actual: $326.0m raised (upsized from $275m), **$78,108k repaid**, **$2.0m of unamortised loan fees written off**, long-term debt $76,521k → $348,077k, interest expense $(326)k → $(26,639)k of which $23,970k non-cash. Unknowns: whether any bank was canvassed for an unsecured revolver; whether a smaller tranche was weighed; the book. [src: s3p2 §N row N-1]

N21 Claim: **Decision — pay for the first acquisitions in stock, and do it offshore** (Bookpages 1998-04-17, Telebook and IMDb 1998-04-24). State before: no acquisition had ever closed; catalogue and fulfilment still one Seattle estate; shares publicly priced for 11 months at $18.00 (pre-split vintage) — Date: 1998-04-17 / 1998-04-24 — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1998-04-17_acc-0000891020-98-000694_filed-1998-05-01.txt` Item 9; 10-K FY1998 L1548-1555; ARS 1998 L120-121 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + RESTATED summary — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] three settlements, 540,066 shares issued on a filed 8-K Item 9 — Conf: High (dates, shares, instrument — settlement dates come from the filed 8-K, not from retrospectives, and **ST3E-F4 holds the folklore attack failed**); **UNKNOWN (price basis)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: **U.163** (~$55m aggregate vs the filed 540,066 restricted shares). Alternatives: build the sites, brand and catalogue in-house; licence the technology layer; pay cash; licence a packaged storefront (the Ingram route Stage 2 §O.2 records). Constraints: Regulation S issuance mechanics; a company with no owned real estate and no retained earnings; the newly acquired payables freedom. Rationale not stated; the founder's letter describes the *result* — "launched … in the U.K. and Germany" under the Amazon brand with Amazon technology. Actual: the first offshore issuance; UK and German stores live by October 1998, six months after the first settlement. [src: s3p2 §N row N-2]

N22 Claim: **Decision — choose the accounting method for each of two acquisitions: Junglee under the purchase method, PlanetAll under pooling of interests** — Date: 1998-08-03 → 1998-08-27 — Source: `sources/8-K_event-1998-08-03_acc-0000891020-98-001210_filed-1998-08-07.txt` (EX-2.1, EX-2.2); `sources/8-K_event-1998-08-12_acc-0000891020-98-001352_filed-1998-08-27.txt` + `sources/8-KA_event-1998-08-12_acc-0000891020-98-001491_filed-1998-10-26.txt`; 8-K event 1998-08-27; 10-K FY1998 L1269-1270, L1603-1612 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT + RESTATED — Passage: "substantially all of the approximately $180 million purchase price allocated to goodwill and other purchased intangibles" — Conf: High (both methods, both closings, every restated delta); **UNKNOWN (who elected, and why)** — the strongest case in the corpus that an accounting choice was made **for** its reporting consequence is inference, not evidence — Corroboration: 1 (**an 8-K and its 8-K/A are one filing restated**) — Conflicts: **N-3 vs §K.2** (K51/L34 family; the operating-cash pair no note explains). State before: two merger agreements signed the same week, Junglee ~4.7m shares, PlanetAll ~2.4m shares, announced aggregate "approximately $280 million". Information available: the purchase/pooling election under GAAP then in force; PlanetAll's equity small and its losses large. Constraints: pooling required retrospective restatement of every period presented; purchase required an allocation amortised over lives averaging ~3 years. Alternatives: elect purchase for both; structure both to pool; use cash earn-outs.
