The figure is a **Q4-over-Q4 like-for-like comparison**, not an undated present tense, which upgrades Stage 2's row from "date unstated" to "dated, and the strongest retention statement in the 1996-98 record" **while leaving its other defects untouched**: "repeat customer" is still undefined, the base is still a share of an undisclosed order total, and no order count is printed. [src: s3p2 §N preamble correction]

N33 Claim: **Instruction-layer correction carried outbound (method §14 rule 4):** `MASTER_RESEARCH_LOG.md` states that "Exchange.com" returns "zero occurrences across all 97 local files" and groups it with three names that genuinely are absent (WarehouseDirect, Internet Mail, Allaire). **This is false** — "Exchange.com" occurs **113 times in 11 files**, including the 8-K headline quoted at N26, Note 7 of the Q1-1999 10-Q, the Q2 and Q3 1999 10-Qs and both FY1999 annual documents, and ST3_A itself quotes that headline — Date: pass of the adversarial review — Source: grep over `sources/`; ST3E-31 — Source date: n/a — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (a defect in another file) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] a dated, priced, HSR-cleared acquisition must not be moved into the folklore column by an agent trusting the log — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: **U.156**; correction issued as OC-203, highest severity of the adversarial pass. **Nothing in this appendix inherits the log's sentence.** [src: s3p2 §N correction]

N34 Claim: §N's knowability verdict, with the evidentiary upgrade stated precisely: **the proportion of `Rationale` cells now sourced to the founder's own in-window words rather than to `RETRO` is the single biggest evidentiary upgrade Stage 3 has over Stage 2, and it is confined to two rows (N-4, N-10) plus the ARS-sourced framing in N-7 and N-9**; everywhere else the rationale remains inferred from what was signed — Date: 1997-05-16 → 1999-12-31 — Source: §N rows; ARS 1997; ARS 1998 — Source date: 1998-04-17; 1999-04-07 — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE (assessment of the section's own evidence) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: none. *NOT KNOWABLE:* which alternatives were weighed, what each was priced at, what was rejected; the reference price behind every stock-funded "value"; the per-target consideration; the terms of the automation contracts (redacted under Rule 24b-2). [src: s3p2 §N knowability]

O. COUNTERFACTUAL OPPORTUNITIES (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

*None below is claimed to have succeeded, none is scored, and none is asserted to have been available on better
terms than the path taken. "Considered" means evidence that someone looked at it, and in this stage that
evidence almost never exists. "Others pursuing it" is limited to routes named in **Amazon's own period
documents**: no competitor filing is in `sources/`, so every competitor cell is Amazon's characterisation of a
rival and is labelled as such (ST3_C's provenance boundary, adopted whole). §2 record-selection warning: the
alternatives are visible because a survivor printed them; failed 1998 and 1999 entrants left no option list —
unarchived, not short.*

O18 Claim: **Counterfactual O.1 — raise equity first and leave the $1.25bn converts unissued.** *Visible:* a $2,000,000,000 universal shelf registered 1999-05-19, i.e. the equity route was opened on record in the same year, and the converts sold 2.5× oversubscribed against the ask in six days — Date: 1999-01-28 → 1999-06-08 — Source: S-3 File No. 333-78797 + S-3A; 8-Ks of 1999-01-28/02-03 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE (counterfactual, unscored) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: Medium (mechanism); **UNKNOWN (menu, and whether an equity block was priced)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. *For:* equity carries no coupon, while the converts added a 4¾% cash obligation from 1999-08-01 (≈$59.4m a year DERIVED) to a business whose trading cash generation was $(320,987)k, struck at a conversion price that never entered the money on any filed 1999 range (M44), so the equity option they sold never relieved the debt. *Against:* dilution at a moment when the founder's voting position was a stated governance asset; the provisional-redemption design explicitly deferred dilution to a 150% gate; and 1999 stock consideration for acquisitions was already $774,409k, so dilution was not a line the company avoided. *Considered:* **no document** — the shelf's existence is capacity, not evidence of a weighed choice. *Others pursuing it:* the only in-record data is the filed note that barnesandnoble.com was "financed by a third party" and that Bertelsmann "purchased a significant interest" — capital from a strategic, not a public issue. [src: s3p2 §O.1]

O19 Claim: **Counterfactual O.2 — buy the long tail without owning it: consignment, drop-ship and marketplace in 1998 rather than 1999.** *Visible:* Amazon's own reason for buying Exchange.com ("ADDING MORE THAN 12 MILLION BOOK AND MUSIC ITEMS … to add hard-to-find" supply), the FY1997 constraint that of >2.5m advertised titles only up to 400,000 were fillable from distributors, and Stage 2's filed "four to six weeks" in-print and "two to six months" out-of-print windows — Date: 1997 → 1999-04-26 — Source: 8-K event 1999-04-26; 424B1 baseline via Stage 2; 10-K FY1999 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: as quoted — Conf: Medium (mechanism, both sides filed); **UNKNOWN (1998 consideration)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. *For:* FY1999 evidence is directional and points this way — inventory consumed $(172,069)k of cash and produced ~$39m of Q4 charges, while the marketplace leg's "1 million registered users and 1.5 million listings" cost the company no inventory at all, and third-party commissions came in inside net sales. *Against:* the filed economics require the first-party shipment for the margin it earns (outbound shipping inside net sales, $239m in 1999; inbound freight inside cost of sales), consignment supply is what Ingram and Book Stacks already controlled at better terms, B&N's pending Ingram purchase is disclosed, and Amazon's filed differentiator was time-to-mailbox. *Others pursuing it:* eBay ("online auction services", person-to-person, "established seller community", no inventory, 10-Q Q1-1999/Q3-1999) and the class Amazon named itself, "online commerce sites that provide goods and services at or near cost, relying on advertising revenues" (FY1998 10-K). [src: s3p2 §O.2]

O20 Claim: **Counterfactual O.3 — phase the network: earn automation experience before ordering it for sites not yet located.** *Visible:* the Q3-1999 10-Q's "no previous experience with automated distribution centers" (L1630-1633) beside the 1999-03-11 Buschman agreements covering Fernley Phases I-II and three undetermined "Site A/B/C" proposals, beside the company's own over-expansion warning — Date: 1999 — Source: 10-Q Q3-1999; 10-Q Q1-1999 EX-10.1-10.5; 10-K FY1999 L1020-1032 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: as quoted at M32 — Conf: Medium (the risk is filed by the company itself); **UNKNOWN (throughput economics either way — no capacity figure exists in the record)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-13. *For:* fulfilment per dollar of sales 8.25% → 11.49% in the year the network scaled; 22.7% of the gross estate still CIP at 1999-12-31; $39m of charges in the first automated peak; the company had conceded in the FY1997 report that it did not know how to size a second site. *Against:* the capacity case is founder-signed and dated; Q4 was 41.4% of FY1998 sales, so capacity arriving after November is worth little in the year it is bought; and the ten-year Fernley lease was signed December 1998, i.e. the phasing decision, if it was one, predates the equipment contracts. *Considered:* the company disclosed the risk in advance, which establishes knowledge, not the weighing of an alternative (Stage 2's O.4 logic, applied). *Others pursuing it:* no in-record rival data-centre or 3PL decision is named; Wal-Mart's only filed appearance is as a plaintiff asserting retail-operations know-how — an adjacent-industry reminder that incumbents owned this capability and Amazon did not. [src: s3p2 §O.3]

O21 Claim: **Counterfactual O.4 — pay cash for fewer targets, or accept a smaller goodwill base.** *Visible:* the $85.4m tenure-contingent maximum recordable as compensation; 1999 stock consideration of $774,409k exceeding the announced ~$645m; Junglee's ~$180m "substantially all" to goodwill amortised over ~3 years, producing $214,694k of 1999 amortisation = 13.1% of net sales; and the audited Early-Stage Businesses and Other segment losing $242.1m on $163.8m of revenue **with a $7.8m gross loss** — before any goodwill charge, the new leg did not cover its own cost of sales — Date: FY1999 — Source: 10-K FY1999 L3307-3319, L2875, Note 14 — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE + DERIVED — Passage: [table composite, figures filed] — Conf: Medium-High (every input audited or company-stated and printed); **UNKNOWN (whether the price was obtainable in cash, and what it would have cost)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M34–M37. *For:* cash discipline would have separated acquisition cost from retention cost on the face of the statements, and the pro-forma block quantifies the gap ($312m wider, company-stated, non-GAAP, never used as audited). *Against:* cash was raised in 1999 and spent on bonds and buildings, not on targets; the targets were private and priced in the only currency a loss-making buyer had; and the tenure condition is evidence that **keeping the people was the deal**, which cash might not have bought at any price. *Others pursuing it:* CDnow and N2K, named by Amazon among "competitive combinations that strengthen their competitive positions", merged — the record's only in-window example of a pure-play choosing consolidation over further buying; barnesandnoble.com took strategic money from Bertelsmann. [src: s3p2 §O.4]

O22 Claim: **Counterfactual O.5 — keep the Senior Loan and grow inside its covenants.** *Visible:* the facility "may be increased to an amount not to exceed $100 million", prepayable at par-plus-fees, its accounts-payable aging covenant policing precisely the line that produced FY1998's positive operating cash; the alternative path was taken and cost $2.0m of unamortised loan fees — Date: 1997-11-07 → 1998-05 — Source: 8-K 1997-11-07; 8-K 1998-04-24; 424B2 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: "may be increased to an amount not to exceed $100 million" — Conf: Medium (mechanism); **UNKNOWN whether any phased alternative was weighed** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. *For:* a secured route retained at a moment when all the new paper was unsecured and subordinated, and covenants that force discipline on supplier float are exactly what M.2 shows the float needed. *Against:* the 50% sweep made the facility a cap on the very financing the company wanted; the notes required no cash interest before 2003-11-01; and the first lien was over substantially all of the assets the company was about to spend $287m a year acquiring. *Considered:* the ordering of the 424B2's own use-of-proceeds sentence — retire the Senior Loan first — is the nearest thing to a stated preference in the corpus, and it is a **stated consequence, not a weighing**. [src: s3p2 §O.5]

O23 Claim: **Counterfactual O.6 — appoint the operating layer when the money arrived, not in September 1999** (and O.7's foreign-entry twin below): the hires are dated 1999-06-23/09-02/09-04 against cash closed 1999-02-03 and a network already in construction — Date: 1999-02 → 1999-09 — Source: 10-K FY1999 exhibit index L4525-4533; ST3E-F2 (no COO and no separate finance chief through FY1998) — Source date: 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] — Conf: Medium; **UNKNOWN (whether any earlier search occurred)** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: none. *Considered:* no document poses the earlier date; the sequencing is visible only from the executed offers. [src: s3p2 §O.6]

O24 Claim: **Counterfactual O.7 — reach foreign customers by export from the US estate, without buying local organisations.** *Visible:* the UK and German stores launched October 1998 with "combined fourth-quarter sales … nearly quadrupled over the third quarter" and "25% of our fourth-quarter 1998 sales … derived from Amazon.co.uk, Amazon.de, and music, video, and gift sales" — that last figure being the source of the "international share" series and **Q4-only and multi-category**, so it does not measure geography alone — Date: 1998-10 → 1999-12-31 — Source: ARS 1998; 10-K FY1998; 10-K FY1999 Note 14 — Source date: 1999-04-07; 1999-03-05; 2000-03-23 — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: as quoted — Conf: Medium-High on the numbers (all audited, one document family); **UNKNOWN on whether export-scale trading could have been sustained, since no per-country cost exists** — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: F70/F71 caption family (M31). *Against:* the segment loss **grew** to $(79,223)k with revenue up 7.7×, so the loss was not a year-one start-up artefact. *Considered:* the choice of acquisition over organic foreign entry is visible in the instruments (Bookpages, Telebook, IMDB settled April 1998; sites launched October 1998); the export-only alternative is never posed in any document → UNKNOWN. *Others pursuing it:* barnesandnoble.com with Bertelsmann, and Yahoo!/AOL portal commerce "either directly or in collaboration with other retailers". [src: s3p2 §O.7]

O25 Claim: **Counterfactual O.8 — go deeper in books instead of wider: the specialist path the SIC code quietly abandons.** *Visible:* classification under SIC 2731 (books publishing) through the IPO, reclassified to retail catalog/mail-order bounded 1998-06-03/1998-06-12 — a filed, datable decision to describe itself as a general retailer; Stage 2's in-window objective "with an initial focus on books"; the depth route still being extended as late as the 500-title programme and the out-of-print service — Date: 1998-06 (classification) → 1999 — Source: H55; S-4 cover pages; 10-K FY1999 L1732-1761, L1890-1892 — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE — Passage: "the introduction of new product lines, particularly toys and electronics" (the margin fall attributed first to breadth) — Conf: Medium — Corroboration: 1 — Conflicts: M-4/U.160 family. *For:* the two filed category facts argue against breadth — music and video margins "are lower than book gross margins" with a "proportionate impact" warning, and the FY1999 gross-margin fall to 17.7% is attributed **first** to the new lines. *Against:* depth in books was already supply-capped at the 400,000 fillable titles with no distributor contract, and B&N's pending Ingram purchase struck at exactly that dependency; and the founder's signed letter frames breadth as the objective ("anything they might want to buy online"), with the risk accepted in the same paragraph. *Considered:* the trade-off was disclosed; the permanent-specialist path is never posed → UNKNOWN. *Others pursuing it:* Book Stacks (CUC-owned) and the niche sites (Stage 1/2 record); CDnow/N2K, a music-only pure-play, which chose to merge; the class row "major store-based retailers" (10-Q Q3-1999). [src: s3p2 §O.8]

O26 Claim: **§O.9 boundary note (not a counterfactual):** what Stage 3 establishes, tested against the same discipline Stage 2 applied to the IPO, is narrower than the growth statistics it contains — by 1999-12-31 the company had audited revenue of $1,639,839k growing 169%, a second and third category year, two foreign operating segments, a filed ten-building estate, $1.576bn of securities sold into the market plus $774,409k of stock handed to targets, ten years of leases signed, and a $39m inventory write-off in its first automated peak. **What it had not established, on any filed number, is the thing Stage 2 could not establish either: that a customer was worth acquiring** — Date: 1999-12-31 — Source: §K–§N rows as registered — Source date: as listed — Tier: 1 — Class: INFERENCE (section verdict) — Passage: [paraphrase — not the filing's wording] Revenue scaled, the loss scaled faster, and the loss was financed by paper and by suppliers — Conf: High — Corroboration: n/a — Conflicts: none. **The dataset still cannot say which of those three facts the founder was optimising, because the only in-window statement of the hierarchy is a shareholder letter, and the letter says "Certainly, some will turn out to be mistakes" without naming one.** [src: s3p2 §O.9]

P. QUANTITATIVE METRICS TABLE (STAGE 3) — CLAIM RECORDS

**Id mapping (method §9.3).** This appendix's §P records run **P91–P205**; the narrative's metric ids run
**P184–P271** (its own continuation of the cross-volume metric series), so the mapping is a constant offset of
**−93** for the metric rows: appendix **P91 = narrative P184**, appendix P92 = P185, … appendix P178 = narrative
P271. Both ids are printed in every cell so neither volume has to be renumbered. Records **P179 onward** are the
§P.2 arithmetic items (**t1–t27**) and the §P.2a corrections and "not computed" list, keyed to those ids.
**Metric-basis discipline carried from Stage 1 §P and Stage 2 §P (method §6, §8, §13):** "net sales" is the
audited revenue line on a shipment basis inclusive of outbound shipping and handling, and from 1999 **also**
zShops/Auctions transaction revenue — an FY1999 "net sales" figure is not the same object as an FY1997 one (a
metric-composition change, not growth, → U.139, U.160); titles "advertised" ≠ "sourceable" ≠ "stocked" ≠
"held"; "customer accounts" from 1999 is expressly "inclusive of accounts with Amazon.com Auctions"; cumulative
series are never used as a denominator; **every restatement pair is printed on both legs and no restated value is
ever printed as if it had been filed.**
**Source keys used in the `Source:` cells below (COR-01 extended to Stage 3):** `10-K405/97` FY1997 Form 10-K405
acc. 0000891020-98-000448, 1998-03-30 · `10-K/98` acc. 0000891020-99-000375, 1999-03-05 · `10-K/99` acc.
0000891020-00-000622, 2000-03-23 · `10-K/A/99` acc. 0000891020-00-001638, 2000-09-08 · `ARS/97`, `ARS/98`
1998-04-17, 1999-04-07 · `Q<n>-<yy>` the Form 10-Q of that quarter · `8-K(<date>)` the Form 8-K whose **event**
date is named · `424B2/98` 1998-08-13 · `424B3/98` 1998-10-22 · `POS AM(<file>)` · `DEF14A/98`, `DEF14A/99`.
**Independence (method §3):** the four annual reports are four states of the issuer's own annual reporting and the
FY1999 10-K and its amendment are **one instrument in two states**; a figure printed in the FY1997 10-K and again
in the FY1998 10-K's comparative column was **filed once by one author twice** — a restatement event, not a
corroboration. Genuinely independent rows in this stage are few: counterparty filings, Media Metrix ratings
relayed inside company paper (relayed, so not independent in the §3 sense), and 8-K-attached releases only where
the release predates the filing that quotes it.
